# Bug audit — 2026-09-13

> **A correctness sweep of the `ui` library core.** Eleven scopes hold 774 files and 57,024 lines. The sweep raised 140 claims. A blind pass judged every claim, and an overturn pass tried to kill each survivor. 106 findings stay open.

The sweep looks for defects only. Style, names, and format stay out of scope, because Biome owns them. A wish for a test, a document, or a refactor is not a defect, and a repository convention is not a defect.

## Scope

The sweep covers the library core of `packages/ui`: `components`, `hooks`, `primitives`, `core`, `utilities`, `providers`, `layouts`, and `types`. It does not cover `modules`, `recipes`, or the demo tree under `src/docs`. The demo tree stays out because the verifier reads it as a consumer root for reach.

Each scope carries an explicit file list. No agent derived a list and no agent widened one. Every file sits in exactly one scope, and the eleven lists sum to the whole set.

| Scope | Theme | Files | Lines | Claims |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `B01` | date and calendar | 34 | 5,569 | 9 |
| `B02` | text, masked and password inputs, form structure | 75 | 5,920 | 15 |
| `B03` | choice, range, colour and capture controls | 74 | 6,269 | 14 |
| `B04` | navigation and command | 79 | 5,122 | 15 |
| `B05` | overlays, feedback, typography and actions | 85 | 4,931 | 7 |
| `B06` | data display, collections | 64 | 5,063 | 16 |
| `B07` | data display leaves and layout surfaces | 123 | 5,535 | 15 |
| `B08` | pdf viewer and filters | 36 | 4,900 | 12 |
| `B09` | hooks | 56 | 5,980 | 12 |
| `B10` | primitives, providers and layouts | 90 | 4,746 | 10 |
| `B11` | core, utilities and types | 58 | 2,989 | 15 |
| **total** | | **774** | **57,024** | **140** |

## Method

The sweep uses four passes for each scope, and one merge pass across the scopes.

1. `bug-sweeper` reads every line of the scope and returns one claim for each defect it can name. It judges nothing.

2. The caller removes the evidence block from each claim. `bug-verifier` therefore sees the id, the file, the symbol, the trigger, the wrong result, and the contract. It sees no line number, no trace, no severity, and no fix idea. The strip runs in code, so the pass is blind in fact and not by request.

3. `bug-verifier` judges the batch from the files. It answers reach against the consumer roots, and it matches each claim against a digest of the nine open audits.

4. `bug-verifier` runs an overturn pass with the sweep evidence attached. It tries to prove each survivor deliberate or already known.

5. A merge pass joins the groups that share a file and a symbol, and orders the steps across all eleven scopes.

Every scope reported full coverage: 774 of 774 files opened, and no file failed to open.

| Pass | In | Out |
|---|---|---|
| sweep | 774 files | 140 claims |
| blind verification | 140 claims | 92 confirmed, 22 narrowed, 24 refuted, 2 restated |
| overturn | 114 survivors | 106 stand, 5 known, 3 deliberate |
| merge | 11 sheets | 25 groups, 72 steps, 54 questions |

## Findings

106 rows. Severity: 14 high, 30 medium, 62 low. Reach: 29 shipped, 7 test-only, 70 none.

Reach asks whether a repository call site executes the trigger. It does not ask whether the code path can execute. A row with reach `none` is a real defect that no shipped surface constructs today.

The `Status` cell takes `◯ OPEN` for a row with no fix, `◐ FIXED` for a fix on a branch, and `✅ RESOLVED ([#NNN](https://github.com/charliebeckstrand/midgard/pull/NNN))` for a merged row ([`CONVENTIONS.md`](../../../../CONVENTIONS.md) §12.4).

## Mechanisms

Each entry below is the specification for its row. `bug-resolver` reads the mechanism paragraph and re-anchors every cited line from its symbol, because the line numbers drift as earlier steps merge.

### `B01-C01` — `DatePickerSingle`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/date-picker/date-picker.tsx`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/components/date-picker/date-picker.tsx:244 `format = 'MM/DD/YYYY',` — DatePickerSingle pins the layout in its own destructure default; :321 `format={format}` hands that concrete string to DateInput on every render. packages/ui/src/components/date-input/date-input-utilities.ts:69 `const resolved = format ?? localeDateInputFormat(locale)` — the locale default only fires when `format` is undefined, which the picker never allows. The relative variant repeats it by omission: packages/ui/src/components/date-picker/date-picker-relative.tsx:160-168 `<DatePicker input clearable={false} value={state.custom.start ?? undefined} …/>` and :172-180 the End field, both `input`-mode pickers with no `format`, so each takes the same :244 default.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated. `<LocaleProvider locale="en-GB"><DatePicker input onValueChange={fn} /></LocaleProvider>` types through maskDateText with format 'MM/DD/YYYY'; packages/ui/src/components/date-input/date-input.tsx:173 `format: (raw) => maskDateText(raw, format)` and :143 `const parsed = parseDateText(text, format)`. The relative Start/End half is reached by opening the relative popover and clicking the "Custom range" row (date-picker-relative.tsx:135 `onClick={state.enterCustom}`).

**Documented intent.** Supports the claim: date-input.tsx:117-118 `// Field order follows the ambient locale unless the caller pins it, so the // masked field agrees with the Calendar it pairs with inside a DatePicker.` — the locale default exists for exactly the DatePicker pairing this file defeats. date-input.tsx:45-47 documents the default as the layout matching the ambient LocaleProvider locale. Sibling test packages/ui/src/__tests__/components/date-input.test.tsx:600-601 `// Day-first: 10 June 2026, not 6 October. The field previously masked // month-first whatever the locale, so this entry read as October.` The picker already follows the locale elsewhere: use-date-picker-state.ts:272 `displayValue: value ? formatDate(value, ambient.locale, ambient.dateFormat) : '',` pinned by date-picker.test.tsx:147-148 `// de-DE writes day-first with dot separators; the trigger followed the // runtime default rather than the provider before.` Conflicts with the claim: date-picker.tsx:42 `@defaultValue 'MM/DD/YYYY'` is an accurate annotation of the code, and the 2026-09-12 doc audit ruled all @defaultValue claims accurate — so the annotation records the behaviour, it does not argue for it, and it contradicts the sibling rationale it pairs with. Not a refutation; it is open question Q2. packages/ui/REFERENCE.md:37 `locale needs an API decision — explicit props or app wrappers — because formats cannot move to CSS.`

**Reach.** Input-mode sites in the roots: packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/date-picker.tsx:23 `<DatePicker input value={typed} onValueChange={setTyped} />` (no format, so it takes the pinned default); packages/ui/src/docs/demos/modules/grid/editable.tsx:121-123 `<DatePicker input format="YYYY-MM-DD" …>` (pins format, unaffected); the relative Start/End shipped default reached from apps/places/src/components/place-filters/place-filters.tsx:148-152 `<DatePicker relative={{ chips: false }} …>` via the Custom range row. On the 1st of a month the two presets resolve equal: :87 `resolve: (now) => ({ from: startOfDay(now), to: startOfDay(now) }),` (today) and :116 `resolve: (now) => ({ from: startOfMonth(now), to: startOfDay(now) }),` (this-month). After both clicks `pickedIds` is `{today, this-month}` (use-date-picker-relative-state.ts:178-181) and the value is two identical spans (:267-271 `const next = presets.filter((option) => selected.has(option.id)).map((option) => option.resolve(now))`), so both spans match `this-month` and `selectedIds` (:394-397) holds one id. date-picker-relative.tsx:111 `const selected = state.selectedIds.has(preset.id)` then drops the Today row's :117 `variant={selected ? 'solid' : 'bare'}` and :119 `aria-pressed={selected}`. ### `B02-C01` — `formReducer ('set-touched' case)`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/form/form-reducer.ts`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/components/form/form-reducer.ts:163 ` case 'set-touched': {`; :168-174 ` const newErrors = runValidators(` / ` action.validate,` / ` state.values,` / ` nextTouched,` / ` action.validateOn,` / ` [action.name],` / ` )` — the fifth argument makes `fields` non-undefined; :62 ` const forced = fields !== undefined`; :73 ` if (forced || validateOn === 'change' || (validateOn === 'touched' && touched[key])) {` — `forced` short-circuits the `validateOn` test, so the validator runs under `'submit'`. The error then reaches the map at :178 ` errors: { ...state.errors, ...newErrors },` and `valid` reads that map at packages/ui/src/components/form/use-form-reducer.ts:175 ` const valid = useMemo(() => !Object.values(errors).some(hasIssues), [errors])`. **Trigger.** Verified as stated: a `<Form validateOn="submit" validate={{…}}>` whose bound control is blurred once. The shipped default is `'touched'` (packages/ui/src/components/form/form.tsx:74 ` validateOn = 'touched',`), which is the one mode the forcing agrees with, so no shipped default constructs the trigger either. Test-only occurrences exist (`__tests__/components/tag-input.test.tsx:662`, `__tests__/boundary/signature-pad.test.tsx:271`) and both pass `'touched'`; marked test-only and out-of-root.

**Severity.** Low. The cost per event is real (a validation error and a false `valid` before submit, in the one mode chosen to prevent exactly that), but `validateOn` is passed by no consumer in any root and the default mode is unaffected.

### `B02-C03` — `formatEditing / groupIntegerPart`

Above 2^53 the round-trip is lossy: `Number('12345678901234567')` is `12345678901234568`. formatEditing returns it at :78 ` let result = (negative ? '-' : '') + groupIntegerPart(intPart, fracPart !== undefined, locale)`, and the component both displays it and re-parses it — packages/ui/src/components/currency-input/currency-input.tsx:120 ` setEditingText(formatted)`, :122 ` const parsed = parseEditing(formatted, group, decimal)`, :127 ` if (parsed !== num) setNum(parsed)`.

**Trigger.** Narrowed one step and verified: any integer run past 2^53 (9007199254740992), i.e. 16 digits in the worst case and every 17-digit run, not only the 17-digit example. Below that the double round-trips exactly and the digits are preserved.

An empty` / `// integer part renders as '0' only when a fraction follows, else stays empty.` — the comment scopes the function to zero-stripping and grouping, both presentation steps. No source states a supported magnitude ceiling.

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/components/toolbar/toolbar-constants.ts:1-12 is a seven-arm selector list: `export const TOOLBAR_ITEM_SELECTOR = [\n\t'a[href]',\n\t'button:not(:disabled)',` … `].join(',')`. The main item query is unsorted: packages/ui/src/hooks/a11y/use-a11y-roving.ts:61-65 `export function queryItems(container: HTMLElement | null, selector: string): HTMLElement[] {\n\tif (!container) return []\n\n\treturn Array.from(container.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(selector))\n}`, reached at :403 `const items = indexed ? [] : queryItems(container, itemSelector)` and :891 `const items = queryItems(el, itemSelector)`. Only the row-controls path sorts, and it records why: :54-56 `// Spec orders selector-list matches by document position, but jsdom\n\t\t\t// (nwsapi) groups them by selector; sort so arrow order is the DOM order.\n\t\t\t.sort((a, b) => (a.compareDocumentPosition(b) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING ? -1 : 1))`. The resting stop reads the same unsorted array: :350 `return bySelector ?? items[0]` (Toolbar passes no `activeSelector`, so `bySelector` is undefined), seated at :897 `seatTabStop(items, resolveRestingStop(items, activeSelector))`. Arrow moves index into it at :713 `: nextIndexForKey(event.key, currentIndex, ctx.items.length, opts)` and :503 `const next = ctx.items[index]`.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated, and the stated environment limit is load-bearing: the defect exists only where the selector engine groups by selector. The repository records that as jsdom/nwsapi (use-a11y-roving.ts:54-55); a spec-conforming `querySelectorAll` returns document order, so a real browser is unaffected. An all-`<button>` toolbar is also unaffected — every button matches the `button:not(:disabled)` arm before the `[tabindex]` arms. The worked example in the claim ("the second press lands on the trailing anchor") depends on the exact arrangement; the grouped-not-DOM order and the mis-seated resting stop are what trace.

### `B04-C13` — `Stepper`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/stepper/stepper.tsx`

**Mechanism.** The flip is real. packages/ui/src/components/stepper/stepper.tsx:16-17 `/** Controlled current step index. Pair with \`onValueChange\`. */\n\tvalue?: number` — the type admits no `null`, so `undefined` is the only clear. :95-99 `const [current = 0, setCurrent] = useControllable<number>({\n\t\tvalue,\n\t\tdefaultValue: defaultValue ?? 0,\n\t\tonValueChange: (next) => onValueChange?.(next ?? 0),\n\t})`. Controlled-ness is decided per render off that one test: packages/ui/src/hooks/use-controllable.ts:32 `const isControlled = value !== undefined` and :34 `const currentValue = isControlled ? (value ?? undefined) : internalValue`. So `value` going `2 → undefined` moves the source of truth to the shadow, and subsequent step clicks write the shadow alone (stepper.tsx:137 `onValueChange: interactive ? setCurrent : undefined` → use-controllable.ts:63 `setInternalValue(normalized)`), with the owner's `value` never agreeing.

**Trigger.** Verified for the flip: a controlled `<Stepper value={step} onValueChange={setStep}>` whose `step` becomes `undefined` silently becomes uncontrolled, and `value?: number` (stepper.tsx:17) gives the owner no `null` alternative — confirming "There is no way to keep a Stepper controlled with no current step".

**Documented intent.** CONVENTIONS.md:77 §7.3 `In value props, \`undefined\` leaves the control uncontrolled; \`null\` keeps it controlled with no current value.` The shared hook already implements both halves and documents them: packages/ui/src/hooks/use-controllable.ts:9-10 `/** Controlled value. \`undefined\` leaves the hook uncontrolled; pass \`null\` to stay controlled with no current value. */\n\tvalue?: T | null`, and the sibling root widens where Stepper does not — packages/ui/src/primitives/current/current.ts:37-38 `/** Controlled active value. \`undefined\` leaves it uncontrolled; \`null\` keeps it controlled with none active (CONVENTIONS §7.3). */\n\tvalue?: string | null`. Stepper's own doc does not claim an exemption.

**Reach.** NONE. Searched apps/admin, apps/places, packages/ui/src/docs/demos, packages/ui/src/modules, packages/ui/src/layouts for `<Stepper\s`. Sites: packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/stepper.tsx:47,69,87,105 (each `value={state}` from a `useState<number>` that never clears) and :127 `<Stepper value={1}>` (a constant display-only readout). No app site clears the value.

**Severity.** Low. The flip is silent and leaves owner and widget disagreeing, but a step index is a value a flow always has, the sibling `Current` root already carries the `null` arm for surfaces that need it, no consumer clears the value, and the 2026-07-13 audit already screened this axis out.

**Prior art.** 2026-07-13-PROPS-AUDIT: `[RULED-OUT] Deliberate architecture screened out before flagging — controlled/uncontrolled triads and \`null\` semantics (§7.3), \`name\` binding (§7.2), recipe variants (§5.2), static-tier explicit sizing and DOM projections, polymorphism (\`href\`/\`render\`), a11y props, \`data-slot\` anchors, and §3.6 per-item render callbacks.` The digest records the §7.3 axis as screened out before flagging, so this claim re-opens ground a prior audit deliberately declined to walk.

### `B04-C15` — `useNavContext / NavContextValue`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/components/nav/context.ts:6-7 `/** Value broadcast by {@link NavContext}: the active \`value\` and its change callback. */\nexport type NavContextValue = CurrentContextValue` and :12-15 `/** Reads the enclosing {@link Nav} selection state ({@link NavContextValue}). */\nexport function useNavContext() {\n\treturn useCurrent()\n}` — the return type is inferred from `useCurrent`. That hook is typed to include `undefined`: packages/ui/src/primitives/current/current.ts:24-27 `export const [CurrentContext, useCurrent] = createContext<CurrentContextValue | undefined>(\n\t'Current',\n\t{ default: undefined },\n)`, and the factory returns a hook of exactly the type parameter: packages/ui/src/core/create-context.ts:47-50 `export function createContext<T>(\n\tname: string,\n\toptions: DefaultOption<T>,\n): [ReactContext<T>, () => T]`, with :64 `function useContextValue(): T {`. `CurrentContextValue` itself is not nullable: current.ts:9-13 `export type CurrentContextValue = {\n\tvalue: string | undefined\n\t/** Fires with the newly active value, or \`null\` once none is active (CONVENTIONS §7.3). */\n\tonValueChange: ((value: string | null) => void) | undefined\n}`. Nothing documents the undefined arm, and `useNavContext` declares no explicit return type at all. Compare the sibling, which names the possibility: current.ts:20 `\`undefined\` outside a provider.`

### `B05-C03` — `HoldButton`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/hold-button/hold-button.tsx`

**Mechanism.** `packages/ui/src/components/hold-button/hold-button.tsx:71-73` — "\t\t<Button" / "\t\t\ttype=\"button\"" / "\t\t\t{...props}" — the literal sits ahead of the spread, so a caller's `type` in `props` replaces it. `type` survives into `props`: the destructure at `hold-button.tsx:41-57` pulls out `duration`, the three hold callbacks, `disabled`, `children`, `className`, the six pointer/key/blur handlers, and nothing else. The prop type accepts it: `hold-button.tsx:12-15` — "export type HoldButtonProps = Omit<" / "\tButtonProps & { href?: never }," / "\t'href' | 'onClick' | 'loading'" / "> & {" — removes only those three keys, and `packages/ui/src/primitives/polymorphic/polymorphic.tsx:23` — "\t| ({ href?: never } & Omit<ComponentPropsWithoutRef<Fallback>, 'className' | Omitted>)" — carries `type` in from `ComponentPropsWithoutRef<'button'>`. Button itself is not the guard: `packages/ui/src/components/button/button.tsx:94` — "\ttype," — destructures `type` out of its own rest, then `button.tsx:178` — "\t\t\t\ttype={type}" and `button.tsx:180` — "\t\t\t\t{...buttonProps}" — writes the caller's value onto `<motion.button>` with no `type` left in `buttonProps` to contest it. `HoldButtonProps` also `Omit`s `onClick`, so no handler is present to call `preventDefault` on the native click a quick press produces.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated: `<form onSubmit={…}><HoldButton type="submit" onHoldComplete={destroy}>Delete</HoldButton></form>`. A pointerdown then pointerup runs `cancel()` at `hold-button.tsx:82-86`, so the hold never completes, while the native click on a `type="submit"` button submits the form.

**Documented intent.** Against it, and named literally. `CONVENTIONS.md:43` — "3.9 Spread order decides what a consumer may override. Load-bearing structural attributes — `role`, `tabIndex`, `type`, widget ARIA state, and the resolved wiring of the §7.2 binding cascade — are written *after* `{...props}`, so a stray prop can't drop a row out of roving, turn a button into a form submit, or clobber a bound field; `menu-item.tsx` is the composite precedent and `switch.tsx` the cascade one." The convention names `type` and names the exact harm. Button's own @remarks confirms the stake — `button.tsx:77-80` — "\t * Mirrors native `<button>` submission semantics: an untyped Button emits no" / "\t * `type` attribute, so the DOM applies its native `submit` default and the" / "\t * Button submits an enclosing `<Form>`/`<form>`. Pass `type=\"button\"` for" / "\t * non-submitting actions and `type=\"reset\"` to reset." No test pins the current order: `packages/ui/src/__tests__/components/hold-button.test.tsx` asserts the slot, the gesture, and the guards, never `type`.

**Reach.** NONE for the stated trigger. Roots searched: apps/admin, apps/places, packages/ui/src/docs/demos, packages/ui/src/modules, packages/ui/src/layouts; term `HoldButton`. Nine in-root sites, none passing `type`: `packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/hold-button.tsx:26,47,60,63,66,74,87`; `packages/ui/src/docs/demos/modules/grid/index.tsx:484`; `packages/ui/src/modules/query/query-builder/query-builder-group.tsx:122`. The shipped default is `type="button"` (`hold-button.tsx:72`), which is correct whenever no `type` arrives — so the component's own wiring does not construct the trigger either. The trigger is nonetheless constructible from any root: `HoldButtonProps` accepts `type` and TypeScript admits it.

**Severity.** Medium. The user cost is high — a destructive-action gate the component exists to enforce is defeated by one quick click, and the failure is silent — but no shipped surface passes `type` today, so reach is latent rather than live.

**Prior art.** NONE for this file. Searched the digest for `hold-button`, `HoldButton`, `type=`, `submit`. The seam is recorded elsewhere: 2026-07-22-API-CONSISTENCY-AUDIT — `[OPEN] checkbox.tsx:100 · radio.tsx:59 — rest is spread last, so a stray checked/onChange clobbers the form binding; Switch spreads rest first and is protected.` Same defect class, different files.

### `B06-C16` — `aggregateAll`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/pivot-table/pivot-table-pivot.ts`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/components/pivot-table/pivot-table-pivot.ts:115-119 `const values: number[] = []` / ` for (const row of groups.values()) for (const bucket of row.values()) values.push(...bucket)` / ` return values.length > 0 ? aggregate(values, op) : undefined`. The spread passes one bucket's whole contents as call arguments, so a bucket larger than the engine's argument limit throws `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` before any aggregation runs. The siblings avoid the spread: :93 `const values = columnKeys.flatMap((col) => groups.get(row)?.get(col) ?? [])` and :105 the same for columns.

**Trigger.** Verified, and broader than stated: `totals` does not gate the computation — packages/ui/src/components/pivot-table/use-pivot-table.ts:87 `const grandTotal = useMemo(() => aggregateAll(groups, aggregation), [groups, aggregation])` runs on every pivot. The failing input is one `(row × column)` bucket above the engine's spread-argument limit; the limit is engine-specific (JavaScriptCore's is far lower than V8's), so the stated ~75,000 throws on some engines and not on others, while a large enough bucket throws everywhere.

**Documented intent.** pivot-table-pivot.ts:110 `/** Aggregates every value in the grid; \`undefined\` when it holds none. */` and use-pivot-table.ts:48 `/** Aggregate over every value in the grid; \`undefined\` when it holds none. */` — a number or `undefined` is the only documented outcome. Nothing bounds the input: use-pivot-table.ts:63 `@remarks Cells recompute when \`rows\`, \`keys\`, or \`aggregation\` change; …` speaks to recomputation cost only, and :98 `// created only when a value is pushed), so no empty-bucket guard is needed.` is the sole size-related note.

**Reach.** `aggregateAll` executes at the shipped demo — packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/pivot-table.tsx:32-37 `<PivotTable` / ` rows={loads}` / ` keys={{ row: 'lane', column: 'period', value: 'loads' }}` / ` rowHeader="Lane"` / ` totals="both"` — but over ten rows (:13-24), so no bucket approaches the limit. No other root builds a PivotTable (searched apps/admin/app, apps/places/src, packages/ui/src/modules, packages/ui/src/layouts).

**Severity.** Low-medium. The failure mode is severe (the table throws instead of rendering) but needs a per-bucket size orders of magnitude beyond anything a shipped root constructs.

**Prior art.** NONE — searched the digest for `pivot`, `aggregate`, `grandTotal`, `RangeError`. Nearest, neither on the aggregation path: 2026-07-22-API-CONSISTENCY-AUDIT `[OPEN] \`pivot-table.tsx:44\` vs \`table.tsx:25\` — PivotTable narrows Table's \`striped\` axis and silently drops \`hover\` and \`bleed\`.` and 2026-07-13-PROPS-AUDIT `[SURFACED] \`PivotTable.totalLabel\` · \`PasswordStrength.labels\` — unused, but each is its component's only i18n hook, to resolve with the T7 locale-provider decision rather than row by row.`

**Dependency.** none — no `node_modules` line cited.

### `B07-C01` — `AccordionTrigger`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/accordion/accordion-trigger.tsx`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/components/accordion/accordion-trigger.tsx:44 `<button`; :45 `type="button"`; :46-47 `// Consumer props spread first; the a11y id wiring, roving tabindex,` / `// context-driven disabled, and data-slot below take precedence.`; :48 `{...props}`; :49 `data-slot="accordion-trigger"`; :50 `{...triggerProps}`. `type` is written BEFORE `{...props}`, so a consumer `type` in the spread bag replaces it. Everything the inline comment names (`{...triggerProps}` at :50, `disabled` at :54, `onClick` at :55, `className` at :59) is written after and is protected; `type` alone is not. The prop is type-legal: :11 `export type AccordionTriggerProps = ComponentPropsWithoutRef<'button'> & {`.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated: an `<AccordionTrigger {...bag}>` inside a `<form>` where `bag` carries an explicit `type`. Constructible from the public prop type; not constructed by any consumer root today.

**Documented intent.** CONVENTIONS.md:43 §3.9 — "Load-bearing structural attributes — `role`, `tabIndex`, `type`, widget ARIA state, and the resolved wiring of the §7.2 binding cascade — are written *after* `{...props}`, so a stray prop can't drop a row out of roving, turn a button into a form submit, or clobber a bound field". The house rule is restated in packages/ui/src/__tests__/components/button-form-submit.test.tsx:11-14 — "Button mirrors native <button>: a typeless button defaults to type=\"submit\" and submits its enclosing form. Every internal control the library renders (steppers, clear/toggle adornments, dismiss buttons) must therefore opt out with type=\"button\", or it would submit whatever form it is dropped into." That file asserts nothing about AccordionTrigger (grep `Accordion|Collapse|Trigger` in it: no matches). No source shows the override is deliberate.

**Reach.** NONE. Searched apps/admin, apps/places, packages/ui/src/docs/demos, packages/ui/src/modules, packages/ui/src/layouts for `AccordionTrigger`. The only sites are packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/accordion.tsx:42, :53, :64, each `<AccordionTrigger>{item.title}</AccordionTrigger>` with no spread and no `type`. No shipped default supplies a `type`: the component's own default at accordion-trigger.tsx:45 is `type="button"` and is correct when no consumer prop arrives.

**Severity.** Low. The wrong result would be severe (an accordion header submits its form), but no root constructs it and the shipped default is correct, so the exposure today is a latent §3.9 violation rather than a live defect.

**Prior art.** 2026-07-22-API-CONSISTENCY-AUDIT — [OPEN] "`checkbox.tsx:100` · `radio.tsx:59` — rest is spread last, so a stray `checked`/`onChange` clobbers the form binding; Switch spreads rest first and is protected." The same spread-order seam is on record for a different pair of files; the digest carries no row naming accordion-trigger.tsx (searched: accordion-trigger, AccordionTrigger, type=\"button\", spread order).

CardHeader adds nothing of its own: packages/ui/src/components/card/card-header.tsx:18 `export const CardHeader = createSlot('div', 'card-header', k.header)`; CardBody likewise, card-body.tsx:15 `export const CardBody = createSlot('div', 'card-body')`.

### `B07-C08` — `loadShiki`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/code/code-block.tsx`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/components/code/code-block.tsx:37 `// Lazy-load shiki on first use.`; :38 `let shikiPromise: Promise<typeof import('shiki')> | null = null`; :47-53 `export function loadShiki() {` / `if (!shikiPromise) {` / `shikiPromise = import('shiki')` / `}` / `return shikiPromise` / `}`. The guard is `!shikiPromise`, so the cell is written once and never cleared; a rejected promise stays memoised for the module's lifetime and every later `loadShiki()` returns the same rejection. The consumer swallows it and paints the fallback forever: :111 `loadShiki()`, :129-133 `// Shiki can fail to load (offline chunk fetch, post-deploy 404) or to` / `// tokenize (a lang/theme outside the bundled set). Keep the plain` / `// fallback that \`setHtml(null)\` already shows rather than leaking an` / `// unhandled rejection and stranding the component with no recovery.` / `.catch(() => {})`, with :143 `{html ? (` … :149 `<pre className={cn(k.block.fallback)} tabIndex={-1}>`. The effect re-runs only on `[code, lang, theme]` (:138) and each re-run hits the same memo cell.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated: the first `loadShiki()` of a session rejects, then any later `<CodeBlock>` mount or remount in the same session. The effect at :94 does re-invoke `loadShiki()` (:111) on every cache miss, so the re-invocation the design assumes happens; it is the memo, not the call site, that blocks recovery.

### `B07-C10` — `AnimatedSparklineMarks`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/sparkline/sparkline.tsx`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/components/sparkline/sparkline.tsx:159-170 — `if (variant === 'bar') {` / `return geometry.bars.map((bar, index) => (` / `<motion.rect` / `key={bar.x}` … `initial={{ y: geometry.baseline, height: 0 }}` / `animate={{ y: bar.y, height: bar.height }}` / `transition={{ ...k.motion.grow, delay: index * k.motion.stagger }}`. The key is the bar's x coordinate, and x is a function of the series length: packages/ui/src/components/sparkline/sparkline-geometry.ts:138 `const slot = innerWidth / data.length`, :140 `const barWidth = Math.max(1, slot - barGap)`, :154 `x: padding + index * slot + (slot - barWidth) / 2,`. Appending one point changes `slot`, so every bar's `x` — and therefore every key — changes; React unmounts every `motion.rect` and mounts a new one, and each new one starts from `initial={{ y: geometry.baseline, height: 0 }}` with its staggered `delay`. The static renderer keys the same way (sparkline.tsx:103 `key={bar.x}`) but has no entrance to replay.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated: a re-render with `data` one element longer, under `animate` and `variant="bar"`. Note the narrower cause than 'any update': a domain change alone (new `min`/`max`, new values at the same length) leaves `x` untouched (geometry.ts:154 depends only on `index`, `slot`, `padding`), so only a length change replays the entrance.

**Documented intent.** packages/ui/src/components/sparkline/sparkline.tsx:52-55 — "Animate the marks in on mount with Framer Motion: the line draws itself (`pathLength`), the area wash fades in behind it, the end-point pops, and bars rise from the baseline in sequence." :143-146 repeats it for the renderer — "The Framer Motion marks: the same shapes, revealed on mount." and :228-229 — "Pass `animate` to reveal the marks on mount through Framer Motion". Nothing documents a replay on data change. The one test that touches the animated bar path asserts only presence: packages/ui/src/__tests__/components/sparkline.test.tsx:60 `it('still renders the marks under animate for both variants', () => {` with :76 `expect(bar.container.querySelectorAll('rect')).toHaveLength(4)`.

**Reach.** NONE. Searched the five roots for `<Sparkline`. Every animated site holds a fixed-length series and remounts by an explicit key on purpose: packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/sparkline.tsx:16 `const series = [4, 6, 5, 9, 8, 12, 11, 15, 14, 19, 22, 20]` with :42-49 `<Sparkline key={runKey} data={series} variant="bar" … animate` and the comment at :18-19 "// The mount animation plays once; a refresh button remounts the sparkline (bumping // its `key`) so the reveal replays on demand."; packages/ui/src/docs/demos/modules/grid/index.tsx:1250-1257 `<Sparkline key={sortKey} data={row.trend} variant="bar" color="green" animate …` over a fixed per-row series. The one polling-shaped site, packages/ui/src/docs/demos/modules/chat.tsx:59-65, passes no `animate`. No root grows a series.

**Severity.** Low. The visible result would be a whole chart flickering back to baseline on each poll, but no shipped surface pairs `animate` with a growing series, and the defect heals on the next render that does not change the length.

**Prior art.** 2026-09-02-SIMPLIFICATION-AUDIT — [RULED-OUT] "`Children.toArray` in the Stepper — reverted, because it drops a `false` child instead of letting it hold its slot, which shifted every stamped key after it and remounted siblings on toggle; both Stepper files walk raw children again." The identical key-instability failure is on record for the Stepper, not for the Sparkline. Searched the digest for sparkline, key, remount, animate; the only sparkline row is 2026-07-22-API-CONSISTENCY-AUDIT [OPEN] "`sparkline.tsx:30` vs `recipes/kata/swatch.ts:23-27` — Sparkline's `variant: 'line'|'bar'` is mark geometry, which Swatch calls `shape`", a naming row.

### `B08-C01` — `usePdfViewer`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/use-pdf-viewer.ts`

**Mechanism.** `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/use-pdf-viewer.ts:344` — " const { rotation, isTransposed, rotate } = usePdfViewerPageRotation(safePage, pagesProp ?? src)". The key is the prop reference itself. `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/use-pdf-viewer-page-rotation.ts:45` — " const prevDocumentKeyRef = useRef(documentKey)"; `:47` — " if (prevDocumentKeyRef.current !== documentKey) {"; `:48` — " prevDocumentKeyRef.current = documentKey"; `:50` — " setRotations({})". A new array identity per parent render fails the `!==` test every render, so the render-phase `setRotations({})` discards `{ [page]: 90 }` and forces React to re-run the component before commit (a fresh `{}` is never `Object.is`-equal, so no bail-out). When provided, drives what's rendered." / " * When omitted, pages are rendered from `src` via pdf.js." — no stability requirement. The sibling prop waives one explicitly at `pdf-viewer.tsx:66-68` — " * @remarks Identity need not be stable: the viewer filters by page and converts at the" / " * point of use rather than memoizing a derived array, so building this inline every" / " * render costs nothing but the on-screen boxes." No test pins rotation persistence across a parent re-render (searched `packages/ui/src/__tests__` for `rotat`, `documentKey`).

**Reach.** NONE among shipped surfaces. Searched apps/admin, apps/places, packages/ui/src/docs/demos, packages/ui/src/modules, packages/ui/src/layouts for `PdfViewer`: one file, `packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/pdf-viewer.tsx`. ### `B08-C04` — `ensureDocumentLoad`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts`

**Mechanism.** `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts:269` — " if (held.snapshot.pages.length > 0 && !held.snapshot.error) return" — an errored partial entry falls through the reuse guard. Then `:274` — " revoke(held.snapshot)", which frees the blob URLs a mounted viewer is rendering (`:102` — " for (const page of snapshot.pages) URL.revokeObjectURL(page.src)"), and `:278` — " publish(src, { pages: [], documentUrl: null, loading: true, error: null })", which pushes zero pages and `loading: true` to every listener on that entry (`:174` — " for (const listener of held.listeners) listener()"). Neither line consults `held.listeners`, unlike the evictor at `:121` — " if (held.loading || held.listeners.size > 0) continue". The subscriber then falls to the skeleton arm at `pdf-viewer-viewport.tsx:86` — " ) : loading ? (".

**Trigger.** As stated: an entry whose first run reported pages and then threw, plus a second `ensureDocumentLoad` for that `src` from a mount while a viewer holds it. The mount path is `use-pdf-viewer-document.ts:253` — " ensureDocumentLoad(src, (report) => rasterizeDocument(src, report))". Verified that the holder is showing a page and not the error arm: with pages present and `loading` false the viewport takes the first arm (`pdf-viewer-viewport.tsx:56`), so the error message is not on screen and the images are live.

**Documented intent.** The opposite rule is stated for the evictor: `pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts:108-111` — " * @remarks Skips anything a viewer is watching, and anything mid-load. A watched entry's blob" / " * URLs are live `<img>` sources, and revoking one blanks the page a reader is looking at — so" / " * the holders outrank the cap…". The retry's own comment assumes no holder: `:272-274` — " // A previous attempt's partial pages, which this run is about to replace. Revoked rather" / " // than left to the evictor: nothing will hold them again, and appending to them would show" / " // the failed attempt's pages twice." The retry itself is deliberate (`:248-250` — "A failure is reported to current subscribers but not remembered: the record keeps its error for them to render, and the next mount retries."); blanking a live holder is the unguarded half, not a stated decision.

**Reach.** Partial. Two viewers on one `src`: NONE — searched apps/admin, apps/places, packages/ui/src/docs/demos, packages/ui/src/modules, packages/ui/src/layouts; the only `src` viewer is `packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/pdf-viewer.tsx:132`, and it is single. The remount arm is reachable at that one site, and any consumer mounting a second viewer on one `src` reaches the full trigger — the cache's own TSDoc names that case as designed-for (`pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts:239-241` — "a second viewer on the same `src` joins the first one's load instead of fetching the file twice"). The partial-then-throw precondition is a property of the document, not of any prop a root can pass.

**Severity.** Medium — a reader's page is revoked out from under a live `<img>` and the viewer restarts a full rasterization, which is exactly the harm the evictor's holder rule exists to prevent; held down by the corrupt-document precondition and by the absence of a two-viewer site in the roots.

**Prior art.** Nearest, not a match: 2026-08-13-EVENTS-AUDIT — "[OPEN] `pdf-viewer.tsx:13` — `onLoad`/`onError` missing; the fetch-rasterize-settle lifecycle is owned at `use-pdf-viewer-document.ts:224,226`…". Pressing "With action" and then "Default" arms 5000 over the live 7000 toast and removes it first with `onDismiss('timeout')`.

**Prior art.** 2026-07-13-PROPS-AUDIT [SURFACED]: "Toast `position` · ToastProvider `duration`/`maxToasts` · CodeBlock `copy` · Combobox `closeOnSelect` · Dialog `placement` · CommandPalette `dismissOnBackdrop` — coherent knobs whose only ever-observed value is the default; kept and recorded so growth is deliberate." ### `B10-C05` — `CurrentContent`

Six call sites, every compute returning an object: packages/ui/src/primitives/option/option.tsx:217 `memoWeak(membershipCache, selectedValue, (values) => new Set(values))`, packages/ui/src/modules/map/engine/map-geometry/cache.ts:103 `memoWeak(decoded, geography, () => new Map())`, :170 `memoWeak(cache, geography, () => new Map())`, :219 `return memoWeak(centroids, features, regionCentroids)` (declared `(LngLat | null)[]` at :218), :296, and packages/ui/src/modules/map/engine/map-geometry/locate.ts:197. `utilities` is not a package entry point — packages/ui/package.json:8-40 exports `./core`, `./hooks`, `./layouts`, `./primitives/*`, `./providers/*`, `./modules/*` and `./*` mapped to components/modules — so apps/admin and apps/places cannot call it at all.

**Severity.** Negligible — no shipped memo stores `undefined`, and the helper is unreachable from the apps; the cost where it did apply is a repeated pure computation.

**Prior art.** NONE. Searched the digest for "memoWeak", "memo", "WeakMap", "cache". The cache rows present (2026-08-02-TEST-SUITE-AUDIT `[RULED-OUT] CodeBlock's html cache cannot flip its assertion`) concern a different cache.

**Dependency.** none — no `node_modules` line cited.

### `B11-C10` — `once`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/utilities/once.ts`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/utilities/once.ts:14-22 `return () => { if (!computed) { value = compute(); computed = true } return value }`. The flag is set after the call returns, so a throwing `compute` leaves `computed` false and the next call runs it again.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated: `once(() => { throw new Error('boom') })` called twice runs `compute` twice.

**Documented intent.** TSDoc packages/ui/src/utilities/once.ts:1-7 "Wraps `compute` so it runs at most once, every later call returning the first result. The lazy seam the charts hang deferred work on ... whichever consumer needs it first ... pays for it off the mount-critical render, with the rest reading the cache." No throw semantics are stated and there is no `once.test.ts` in packages/ui/src/__tests__/utilities.

**Reach.** Eight call sites, all pure derivations or a DOM reclaim: packages/ui/src/utilities/resolve-locale.ts:11 `once(() => new Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().locale)`, packages/ui/src/utilities/print-frame.ts:42 `const cleanup: () => void = once(() => { window.removeEventListener('focus', cleanup); iframe.remove() })`, packages/ui/src/modules/chart/choropleth-chart/choropleth-chart.tsx:238, packages/ui/src/modules/chart/scatter-chart/scatter-chart.tsx:207, packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/use-chart-cartesian.ts:914, packages/ui/src/modules/chart/sector-chart/sector-chart.tsx:242, packages/ui/src/modules/chart/heatmap-chart/heatmap-chart.tsx:573, packages/ui/src/modules/map/map-plat.tsx:930. `packages/ui/src/modules` is a root, so these are root sites; none of the thunks mutates state outside its own return value and none is documented to throw.

**Severity.** Low — the retry-recompute is certain, but every shipped thunk is a pure derivation, so the cost is a repeated computation after a failure that already surfaced, not a doubled side effect.

**Prior art.** NONE. Searched the digest for "once", "at most once", "lazy", "thunk". The nearest row, 2026-09-02-SIMPLIFICATION-AUDIT `[RULED-OUT] primitives/mount/held-motion heldMotionProps — it is reached at its module path, not through the mount barrel`, is unrelated.

**Dependency.** none — no `node_modules` line cited.

### `B11-C11` — `isScrollbarPress`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/utilities/scrollbar-press.ts`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/utilities/scrollbar-press.ts:37-41 `const onVerticalScrollbar = canScrollY && (style.direction === 'rtl' ? event.offsetX <= target.offsetWidth - target.clientWidth : event.offsetX > target.clientWidth)`. `offsetWidth - clientWidth` is both border widths plus the scrollbar width, while the LTR arm compares against `target.clientWidth` alone and carries no border term. `offsetX` is measured from the padding edge, so on a bordered RTL scroller every press from the end of the gutter up to the gutter width plus the two border widths — plus the boundary pixel that `<=` admits — is reported as a gutter press.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated: a press a few pixels inside the inline-start content edge of a bordered, vertically scrolling element under `direction: rtl`.

**Documented intent.** TSDoc packages/ui/src/utilities/scrollbar-press.ts:15-17 "@remarks An axis is only tested where it can actually scroll, so a bordered box that does not overflow has no gutter to press. The vertical gutter sits on the inline-start edge under `direction: rtl`, which is why the test is not simply 'past the content box on the right'." The RTL edge is deliberate; the border term is not documented, and the doc names the bordered box as a case the helper means to get right.

**Reach.** Two call sites, one of them heavily travelled: packages/ui/src/hooks/use-floating-ui.ts:354 `(target instanceof HTMLElement && isScrollbarPress(event, target))` on the outside-press dismiss path, and packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-highlights.tsx:399 `if (target === viewport && isScrollbarPress(event, viewport)) return`. The RTL half is reached by NONE of the roots: searched apps/admin, apps/places and packages/ui/src/docs/demos for `rtl` — no matches.

**Severity.** Low — the arithmetic error is certain and sits on the dismiss path every floating surface uses, but no shipped surface sets `direction: rtl`, so it cannot fire today; it would rise to medium the day an RTL locale ships.

**Prior art.** NONE. Searched the digest for "scrollbar", "isScrollbarPress", "gutter", "rtl", "direction". The `direction` rows found (2026-07-22 `flex.tsx`/`stack.tsx` `direction`, `shiny-text.tsx:49`) are prop-naming rows.

**Dependency.** none — no `node_modules` line cited.

### `B11-C12` — `keyByOccurrence`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/utilities/key-by-occurrence.ts`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/utilities/key-by-occurrence.ts:15 `return { key: occurrence === 0 ? value : \`${value}\u0000${occurrence}\`, value }`. The synthesised key lands in the same namespace as a pass-through value, so `['a','a','a\u00001']` yields keys `['a','a\u00001','a\u00001']` — the second entry's synthesised key equals the third entry's first-occurrence key.

**Trigger.** Verified exactly as stated.

**Documented intent.** TSDoc packages/ui/src/utilities/key-by-occurrence.ts:1-5 "Pair each string with a React-key-safe identifier ... Lists with duplicates (controlled tag values, un-deduped validation messages) collide on bare value keys." Uniqueness is the whole purpose. packages/ui/src/__tests__/utilities/key-by-occurrence.test.ts:32 checks uniqueness for `['x','x','x']` only; no test feeds a value containing the separator.

**Reach.** Three call sites, all fed by consumer data: packages/ui/src/components/tag-input/tag-input.tsx:201 `const keyedTags = keyByOccurrence(tags)`, packages/ui/src/components/fieldset/message.tsx:126 `const keyed = keyByOccurrence(issues)`, packages/ui/src/components/address-input/address-input.tsx:153. The root that supplies values is packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/tag-input.tsx:12 `<TagInput value={tags} onValueChange={(v) => setTags(v ?? [])} placeholder="Add a tag" />`; apps/admin and apps/places render no TagInput (searched both for `TagInput`, `CopyButton`). No root produces a NUL-bearing string.

**Severity.** Negligible — the collision is certain but needs a value holding U+0000 followed by the matching index; no root, and no keyboard path, produces one.

**Prior art.** NONE. Searched the digest for "keyByOccurrence", "key", "duplicate", "React key". The nearest row, 2026-09-02-SIMPLIFICATION-AUDIT `[RULED-OUT] Children.toArray in the Stepper — reverted, because it drops a false child instead of letting it hold its slot, which shifted every stamped key after it and remounted siblings on toggle`, concerns key stamping in another component.

**Dependency.** none — no `node_modules` line cited.

### `B11-C13` — `resolveFormat`

**File.** `packages/ui/src/utilities/format.ts`

**Mechanism.** packages/ui/src/utilities/format.ts:144 `if (spec.type === 'id') return (value) => formatId(value, spec)` — the `id` branch never reaches `Intl.NumberFormat`. packages/ui/src/utilities/format.ts:119-127: `const whole = Math.trunc(value)`; `const sign = whole < 0 ? '-' : ''`; `const digits = Math.abs(whole).toString()`; `const body = spec.pad ? digits.padStart(spec.pad, '0') : digits`; `return spec.prefix ? \`${spec.prefix}${spec.separator ?? '-'}${sign}${body}\` : …`. With `pad: 4`, NaN gives `digits = 'NaN'` (length 3) and `body = '0NaN'`, so the output is `'INV-0NaN'` — one zero, not the claim's `'INV-00NaN'`, which would need `pad: 5`. Infinity gives `'INV-Infinity'` exactly as claimed.

**Trigger.** Verified as stated: `resolveFormat({ type: 'id', prefix: 'INV', pad: 4 })(Number.NaN)` and the same formatter on `Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY`.

**Documented intent.** @remarks packages/ui/src/utilities/format.ts:137-138 "Non-finite values format as `Intl` renders them (`NaN`, `∞`); a caller that wants a gap glyph guards before calling." The `id` branch cannot honour it, because it bypasses `Intl` entirely. packages/ui/src/__tests__/utilities/format.test.ts:73-89 covers finite ids only (`INV-10`, `INV-0007`, `SKU_3`, `42`, `-005`).

**Reach.** NONE shipped. Searched apps/admin, apps/places, packages/ui/src/docs/demos, packages/ui/src/modules, packages/ui/src/layouts and the whole package for `type: 'id'` — the only occurrences are the TSDoc example at packages/ui/src/providers/locale/use-format.ts:23 and the tests at packages/ui/src/__tests__/providers/use-format.test.tsx:48 and packages/ui/src/__tests__/utilities/format.test.ts:73-89 (test-only sites, marked as such).

**Severity.** Low — a public spec whose documented non-finite behaviour is wrong for one of its two branches, but no shipped surface uses the `id` spec, so no cell renders the bad string today.

**Prior art.** NONE. Searched the digest for "resolveFormat", "format", "Intl", "NaN", "useFormat". The format rows present concern `PivotTable.totalLabel` i18n and locale-provider decisions, not this spec.

**Dependency.** none — no `node_modules` line cited.

## Root-cause groups

25 groups. A group holds the rows that one decision closes. A row outside every group is independent.

**G1** (`B01`) — DatePickerSingle's `input` branch hands DateInput an incomplete prop set: it pins `format` over DateInput's locale resolution and never forwards the `invalid` the hook already resolved. Members: `B01-C01`, `B01-C03`.

**G2** (`B01`) — All three DatePicker state hooks spend `readOnly` on `setOpen` alone: no value writer reads it, the trigger clear has no readOnly term, the input branch never forwards it, and a controlled `open` bypasses even the open gate. Members: `B01-C02`, `B01-C04`, `B01-C05`, `B01-C06`.

**G3** (`B01`) — The relative variant identifies a committed span only by re-resolving it to a preset, so two spans that resolve equal collapse onto one preset id in the highlight and in the chip keys. Members: `B01-C07`, `B01-C08`.

**G4** (`B02`) — Message's two return branches disagree on the prop spread: the `<ul>` branch spreads nothing and the `<p>` branch spreads after `role`, so one public native surface is dropped on one branch and clobbers a load-bearing `role` on the other. Members: `B02-C07`, `B02-C08`.

**G5** (`B02`) — Input and Textarea do not destructure `defaultValue`, so it survives the props Omit and lands in `...rest` on the element beside the bound `value` the §7.2 cascade supplies. Members: `B02-C09`, `B02-C10`.

**G6** (`B03`) — RadioGroup and CheckboxGroup write a hard-coded `role` before `{...props}`, inverting CONVENTIONS.md §3.9 so a consumer key of the same name wins. Members: `B03-C13`, `B03-C14`.

**G7** (`B03`) — RangeSlider takes the `name` binding but not the rest of the control cascade its single-thumb sibling completes: `setTouched` is never called and `invalid` never reaches `invalidAttrs`. Members: `B03-C06`, `B03-C07`.

**G8** (`B03`) — `useRangePointer`'s `onPointerDown` diverges from `useColorDrag`, the model its own comment names: `preventDefault()` with no compensating synchronous focus, and a `disabled` guard with no `event.button !== 0` test. Members: `B03-C04`, `B03-C05`.

**G9** (`B02`, `B03`) — MERGED (B02-G4 + B03-G4). One resolved Control identity reaches elements that are not the field's control: all three rows quote use-control-props.ts:89 `id: input.id ?? control?.id,` (with :73 describedBy and :92 required) and input.tsx:83 `useIdScope({ id: sharedAttrs.id })`, against label.tsx:40 `htmlFor={htmlFor ?? control?.id}` — Control broadcasts one generated id to every descendant, and two presentational `<Input>` sub-parts adopt the enclosing Field's id, required and describedby. Members: `B02-C11`, `B03-C10`, `B03-C11`.

**G10** (`B04`) — Resolved structural wiring (`type`, widget ARIA state, derived ids, roving handler) is written before `{...props}` in six navigation and disclosure parts, the inverse of CONVENTIONS.md §3.9. Members: `B04-C01`, `B04-C04`, `B04-C07`, `B04-C08`, `B04-C09`, `B04-C10`.

**G11** (`B04`) — The anchor branch of a polymorphic item omits a guard its own button branch carries: MenuItem's link row gets no `onKeyDown`, CommandPaletteItem's disabled link keeps its `href`. Members: `B04-C02`, `B04-C14`.

**G12** (`B05`) — One `<Button>` call in hold-button.tsx sits on the wrong side of its own spread in both directions: load-bearing `type` before it, presentational `data-slot` after it. Members: `B05-C03`, `B05-C04`.

**G13** (`B05`) — The sheet kata's side-blind bases were authored for a side-docked panel: the `sm:max-w-*` cap and the handle's `absolute inset-y-0` survive into the `top`/`bottom` arms. Members: `B05-C05`, `B05-C06`.

**G14** (`B06`) — Listbox resolves the full control state through `useControlProps` but applies only part of it: the open guard reads `resolvedReadOnly` and never `resolvedDisabled`, and the spreadable `validation` object is left on the floor. Members: `B06-C06`, `B06-C07`.

**G15** (`B06`) — The virtualized JsonTree derives its expanded set from one-shot seeds that never re-derive, and the seeding setter reports a change even when the union adds nothing. Members: `B06-C12`, `B06-C14`.

**G16** (`B07`) — Three static leaves write a §3.9 load-bearing attribute before `{...props}`: `type="button"` on both disclosure triggers and `role`/`aria-orientation` on Divider. Members: `B07-C01`, `B07-C04`, `B07-C13`.

**G17** (`B07`) — Both disclosure triggers gate `aria-controls` on `open` rather than on panel presence, each carrying the same false comment, and neither calls the exported `mountsEveryPanel`. Members: `B07-C02`, `B07-C03`.

**G18** (`B07`) — AvatarGroup reaches its children only through CSS descendant selectors, so the Avatar status branch breaks the projection twice: the ring lands on the status wrapper and the StatusDot size is a React prop no selector can set. Members: `B07-C06`, `B07-C07`.

**G19** (`B08`) — The two pure pdf-viewer arithmetic helpers exported for the jsdom test seam are the only documented, unbarrelled symbols in their files with no `@internal` tag, and no gate scans `components` for the omission. Members: `B08-C11`, `B08-C12`.

**G20** (`B08`) — The document cache reports a wrong state to a live subscriber on two transitions: a subscribed-but-unstarted `src` reports absent rather than loading, and a retry on an errored entry revokes a snapshot a viewer is holding. Members: `B08-C02`, `B08-C04`.

**G21** (`B08`) — The viewer has no single document identity, so the two per-document reset hooks each invent their own key and each gets it wrong in a different direction. Members: `B08-C01`, `B08-C06`.

**G22** (`B08`, `B09`) — MERGED (B08-C03 + B09-C05). `useA11yAnnouncements` advances its dedupe baseline only on the announcing path: both rows quote use-a11y-announcements.ts:30 `const previous = useRef(message)`, :35 `if (!message || message === previous.current) return` and :37 `previous.current = message`, so a `null` pass leaves the baseline at the old string and the same string returning is swallowed. Members: `B08-C03`, `B09-C05`.

**G23** (`B10`) — ToastProvider hands one shared object where its own documentation makes the contract per-toast: one remaining/timer pair serves the whole stack, and one two-parameter `dismiss` closure sits on both the reason-taking and the reason-free context. Members: `B10-C03`, `B10-C08`.

**G24** (`B11`) — The shared live-region write path in `announce` holds one module-scope node and mutates it inside a single task, so it neither notices the node left the document nor gives the accessibility tree a rest state between two identical messages. Members: `B11-C02`, `B11-C03`.

**G25** (`B11`) — Three one-function helpers state a guarantee their implementation drops at one edge: no runtime freeze behind "frozen singletons", `undefined` doubling as the WeakMap miss sentinel, and the at-most-once flag set after a call that can throw. Members: `B11-C07`, `B11-C09`, `B11-C10`.

## Recommended resolution

72 steps, in the order the merge pass set. A step names the change, the rows it closes, the files it touches, and the steps it depends on. `bug-resolver` closes one step for each invocation.

A step with a gate question waits. The reader settles the question first ([`CLAUDE.md`](../../../../CLAUDE.md) §1.3).
