## Open questions

54 questions. The reader settles each one before the steps that name it can run. Each entry gives the decision axes and what each outcome changes.

**Q1 (merges B04-Q1 and B05-Q3) — Is a leaf's `data-slot` presentational and renameable, or structural once a selector keys on it?**

*Axes.* CONVENTIONS.md §3.9 says both: load-bearing attributes go after the spread, and a leaf's `data-slot` default is destructured so a consumer can rename the anchor. Sidebar's roving selector keys on `[data-slot="sidebar-item-inner"]`, and §10.2 makes `data-slot` the query surface of every component test. Second axis: per-component or package-wide.

*Changes.* Structural: S5 moves `data-slot` after the spread in nav-item and sidebar-item and the C08 `data-slot` arm closes; S1's Divider half and S4's destructure become the exception that needs stating. Presentational: S1, S4 and S6 keep the destructured default, the C08 `data-slot` arm goes to the ruled-out list with §3.9's second half as its guard, and Sidebar's roving needs a selector that does not depend on a renameable anchor.

*Settled.* An anchor locks only where the library reads it. A leaf whose `data-slot` the library itself selects on — a roving `itemSelector`, or a kata `has-[]` rule — writes it after `{...props}`; every other leaf writes it before the spread, so a wrapper can re-anchor the leaf it renders. [`CONVENTIONS.md`](../../../../CONVENTIONS.md) §3.9 carries the rule.

The package-wide answers both fail on evidence. Renaming is the library's own composition idiom at 28 sites — `toolbar-separator.tsx` re-anchors `Divider`, eight wrappers re-anchor `Input`, three re-anchor `Flex` — and each leaf writes the anchor before the spread to permit it, so a package-wide lock breaks all 28. A package-wide rename leaves Accordion's and Sidebar's roving keyed on anchors a consumer can take away.

S1's Divider half therefore needs no `data-slot` move and no destructure: `divider.tsx` is already on the right side of the rule. `collapse-trigger.tsx` locks an anchor no selector reads and moves its anchor above the spread. The `B04-C08` `data-slot` arm stands, because `sidebar-item-inner` is read by `sidebar.tsx` and written before the spread.

**Q2 (merges B04-Q2 and B05-Q4) — When a consumer supplies the same key as the component, do the two compose or does one lose?**

*Axes.* Handlers: sidebar.tsx runs roving first then the consumer's; menu-item-utilities runs the consumer's first and skips activation when already `defaultPrevented`. Styles: ShinyText's own file composes the consumer's hover handler but replaces the style object, and only three style keys carry the effect. The axis is whether a consumer may opt out of the component's behaviour for one key.

*Changes.* Compose: S5's tab-list edit is a two-line compose with a `defaultPrevented` gate and a suppressed-case test; S7 spreads `props.style` with the three effect keys last. Component-wins: S5 runs roving unconditionally and S7 becomes a plain reorder below the spread that silently drops a caller's unrelated style keys.

**Q3 (merges B02-Q5, narrowed to its id axis, and B03-Q3) — Who may adopt the resolved Control identity: is a `Control` a single-field or multi-field scope, and how does a presentational sub-part opt out of the cascade?**

*Axes.* control.tsx broadcasts to "control-aware descendants" in the plural, while `<Field>` is the documented one-control wrapper and all 15 Control sites in the roots hold one control-aware descendant. For the sub-parts: per-call-site explicit props, a documented escape on `Input` itself, or a headless wrapper as ColorPickerTrigger uses.

*Changes.* Single-field plus per-call-site opt-out: S20 stays at control.tsx, use-control-props.ts and the two sub-part files. Multi-field: S20 derives a per-descendant id, which changes what `useControlProps` and `useIdScope` each own and needs a rule for which descendant `<Label htmlFor>` names. An escape on `Input` pulls the public Input export, its TSDoc and the matching docs index into S20 under CLAUDE.md §3.5 and widens the blast radius to every Input consumer.

**Q4 (B02-Q3) — Is `defaultValue` removed from Input and Textarea unconditionally, or only while the control is form-bound?**

*Axes.* An unbound control legitimately uses native `defaultValue`, and four fieldset demo sites do. The bound test already exists inside `useInputValue`.

*Changes.* Unconditional (adding it to the props Omit): the type stops admitting the prop and the four demo sites move to `value`. Bound-only: S19 threads `bound` out of `useInputValue`, which is why that file is in its set, and the unbound sites are untouched.

**Q5 (merges B04-Q7 and B10-Q3) — Does the §7.3 `null` arm survive the controlled contract, and where does `null` stop collapsing to `undefined`?**

*Axes.* `useControllable` folds `null` deliberately and says so, and it backs every controlled component; Stepper additionally coerces with `?? 0` so its public callback never emits `null`; `CurrentContextValue.value` and the `contextValue === undefined` arm are local to one primitive. Weigh contract uniformity against the semantics of "a stepper with no current step" and against a breaking callback widening.

*Changes.* Fix the hook: S38 and S39 both shrink to their call sites but every `useControllable` consumer enters the blast radius, including the §7.2 binding cascade. Fix per component: S39 stays inside `primitives/current` and S38 widens Stepper's `value`/`onValueChange` and must answer what a `null`-valued Stepper renders. Exempt Stepper: S38 becomes a doccomment naming the exemption.

**Q6 (B01-Q1) — Under `readOnly`, does the DatePicker trigger's clear affordance disappear, or stay rendered and inert?**

*Axes.* DateInput already hides it under `readOnly`, and the trigger declares a Listbox/Combobox mirror; against that, the clear returns focus to the trigger as it unmounts, which changes the focus story for a control `readOnly` deliberately keeps focusable.

*Changes.* Hide: the gate lands in `DatePickerTrigger.showClear` behind a new `readOnly` prop and S24 keeps date-picker-trigger.tsx. Inert: the gate lands only in the hooks' `handleClear`, S24 drops that file, and a button that looks actionable and does nothing becomes accepted.

**Q7 (B01-Q2) — Does `DatePicker`'s `format` keep its pinned `'MM/DD/YYYY'` default, or defer to the ambient locale?**

*Axes.* The `@defaultValue` annotation is accurate and published; against it, DateInput's comment names the DatePicker pairing as the reason the locale default exists, the trigger label already reads the ambient locale, and REFERENCE.md records locale as an open API decision.

*Changes.* Defer: S25 drops the destructure default and the typed field agrees with the calendar under any locale. Keep: the row becomes a documentation conflict, S25 narrows to the `invalid` forward, and only the relative Start/End fields need a locale-derived format.

**Q8 (B01-Q3) — May the relative value hold two spans that resolve equal, or must a commit dedupe them?**

*Axes.* The utilities' doc says multi-select rebuilds "stable and deduped", but the value is consumed as an opaque span list by apps/places and the URL codec can hydrate a duplicate with no picks behind it.

*Changes.* Dedupe on commit: the element type is unchanged and S41 derives the highlight from the picks. One entry per pick: each entry carries a preset id, so the array element type changes and S41 reaches apps/places' imports and its URL codec.

**Q9 (B02-Q4) — Does `set-touched` gain the `validateOn === 'submit'` early return, or does `runValidators` split "restrict to" from "force"?**

*Axes.* The `fields` parameter conflates the two meanings today and `handleSubmit` depends on the force. The guard fixes one call site; the split fixes the parameter and changes both.

*Changes.* Guard: S26 is a three-line change confined to form-reducer.ts. Split: S26 also edits `handleSubmit` and the signature, and the existing table cases that exercise `fields` under `'submit'` are re-expressed.

**Q10 (B02-Q6) — Should `useMaskInput` format the controlled `value`, given the formatted text then disagrees with the consumer's state until the next round-trip?**

*Axes.* Format-on-read honours the TSDoc but shows text the consumer does not hold; format-on-write only keeps the two in step and makes the TSDoc false for the controlled arm; a third option states the pre-format requirement for `value` as the file already does for the Form binding.

*Changes.* Format-on-read: S55 edits one expression and both root controlled demos keep working. Format-on-write: S55 becomes a TSDoc correction and the row closes as a documentation defect.

**Q11 (B03-Q1) — Which element takes DOM focus on a RangeSlider pointer press, and when does the stacked pair defer?**

*Axes.* Focus the thumb resolved at pointerdown, focus at the later resolve moment inside `resolveDraggingThumb`, or focus the track and leave the thumbs keyboard-only.

*Changes.* The first two require the thumb refs to reach `useRangePointer`, which keeps range-slider.tsx in S9's file set with a `thumbRefs` option mirroring the keyboard hook. The third keeps S9 to one file but does not restore the documented slider handoff, closing one row and leaving the other open.

**Q12 (B03-Q2) — What counts as blur for a two-button widget with no native input?**

*Axes.* A root-level `onBlur` with a `relatedTarget` containment test, an `onBlur` on each thumb, or marking touched at the end of a pointer drag as well.

*Changes.* The first adds one handler to the root; the second adds two plus a guard so moving between thumbs does not read as leaving; the third puts `setTouched` in the pointer path, which pulls use-range-pointer.ts into S23 and re-opens the S9/S23 ordering.

**Q13 (B03-Q4) — Does a clear on an already-empty signature pad still mark the field touched?**

*Axes.* Gate `setTouched` on `commitStroke` reporting a real commit, gate on the drawing ref exposed to the wrapper, or leave `clear` unconditional and gate only `commit`.

*Changes.* The first changes `commit`'s return type and keeps both files in S27. The third keeps the drawing hook out of the file set but leaves an imperative `clear()` marking a never-drawn pad touched, which an existing boundary test relies on.

**Q14 (B03-Q5) — Where does the `indeterminate` restore live?**

*Axes.* Drop the effect's dependency array so it re-asserts on every commit, re-assert inside the change handler, or both.

*Changes.* The first is one character but runs a DOM write on every render of every checkbox. The second must compose with the resolved change handler from `useFormToggle`, so Checkbox grows a wrapper handler.

**Q15 (B03-Q6) — Is ColorPanel's `value` a strict controlled prop or a seed-and-report?**

*Axes.* Strict §7.2 ("an explicit `value` prop wins") with re-derivation every render, today's report-and-drift, or an intermediate that snaps back only when the incoming value differs from the last emission.

*Changes.* Strict makes S57 a real behaviour change and the echo guard must be re-thought against drag frequency. Report-and-drift makes S57 documentation-only, which §5.5 bars me from specifying, so the step closes with no code change. The intermediate may make S57 a no-op.

**Q16 (B04-Q3) — How does an anchor branch express "disabled", and how does a link row answer Space?**

*Axes.* MenuItem removes the anchor entirely; CommandPaletteItem keeps it and guards only the click. For Space, an anchor natively answers Enter only, so the row must synthesise a click (which navigates) or `preventDefault` and call `onAction` directly (which does not).

*Changes.* Anchor-removal converges the two components. Href-stripping leaves a focusable anchor with no destination that needs its own Enter story. Click-synthesis matches the dropdown exactly; direct activation diverges from it and must be documented in MenuItem's TSDoc.

**Q17 (B04-Q5) — When a `SidebarItem` carries both an explicit `suffix` and a `SidebarItemActions` child, what renders?**

*Axes.* The two doccomments together delete the subtree, which neither states. Does the losing content fall back into the inner button (where the file warns an interactive control breaks the markup), take the slot anyway, or become a development-time error?

*Changes.* Inner fallback: S37 is a one-condition change plus a doc sentence, but interactive controls then nest inside the button. Actions-win: the doccomments invert and consumers relying on "suffix wins" change behaviour. Documented error: S37 becomes a dev warning and nothing renders differently in production.

**Q18 (B04-Q6) — Does the document-position sort go into `queryItems` for every roving consumer, or only where the selector is a list?**

*Axes.* `queryItems` runs on the arrow auto-repeat path and the hook already skips it for indexed mode to avoid the per-keystroke allocation; only Toolbar passes a multi-kind selector today. Weigh blast radius against per-keystroke cost, and whether a jsdom-only artefact justifies a hook-wide change.

*Changes.* Hook-wide: S33 is one edit and every future list selector is safe. Toolbar-only: S33 sorts at the call site or flattens the selector, leaving the hook untouched. No change: the row is accepted as a jsdom artefact and recorded as a test-authoring caveat.

**Q19 (B05-Q2) — Is `side="top"|"bottom"` a supported shipped surface, or speculative surface to narrow away?**

*Axes.* No consumer root constructs either arm and only the component test exercises them; against narrowing, the digest keeps the axis, the kata ships classes for all four sides, and the resize gesture keys on all four.

*Changes.* "Supported" makes S54 the fix as written. "Speculative" turns S54 into a removal of the two arms from the kata, the prop type and the tests, adding sheet.tsx and the docs index to its file set.

**Q20 (B05-Q1) — Should a `width` on a cross-docked sheet be neutralized at runtime, or made unrepresentable in the type?**

*Axes.* Whether the TSDoc sentence is a runtime guarantee the recipe must produce or an API contract the type should enforce, and whether a breaking narrowing of `SheetProps` is acceptable in this pass. Only live if Q19 answers "supported".

*Changes.* Runtime keeps S54 at the recipe plus its tests. Type adds sheet.tsx and the matching docs index under CLAUDE.md §3.5.

**Q21 (B06-Q1) — Where does the Listbox's warning/success ring paint: on the trigger button, or on the ControlFrame that draws the border?**

*Axes.* Which element owns the visible ring in the recipes for a select trigger versus a bare input; whether `SelectTrigger` should accept the attribute object at all, given Combobox shares it.

*Changes.* On the button: S22 stays inside the two listbox files. On the frame: it adds the shared select-trigger primitive and makes the change shared with Combobox, widening the review beyond this scope.

**Q22 (merges B06-Q2 and B06-Q5) — How does the package count and walk rendered children, given the reverted Stepper `Children.toArray` precedent?**

*Axes.* For KanbanColumnBody the question is React's own child walk against a hand-rolled filter over the raw array, where the body only counts and renders children verbatim. For `stampTreePositions` it is descent into Fragments only against any non-element container, where the position value's TSDoc defines a level, not a DOM depth.

*Changes.* Hand-rolled count plus Fragment-only descent keeps S47 at one line and S44 inside tree-item-children.tsx, matching the quoted contracts. Adopting `Children.toArray` re-opens what the Stepper reversal settled; a general descent must also define what a wrapper component contributes to `setsize`, reaching tree-item-content.tsx.

**Q23 (B06-Q3) — Does the reorderable row's Tab stop follow `activates`, or a real focusable-descendant probe?**

*Axes.* `activates` is a prop-time derivation and cannot see a focusable child, which is the shape the grid column manager ships; a probe needs a measurement and a render pass. The remarks promise exactly one stop per row either way.

*Changes.* Following `activates` is one line in list-item.tsx and leaves the focusable-child case open. A probe closes both but adds an effect to every reorderable row and folds a lead into this scope.

**Q24 (B06-Q4) — Should the read-only key fallback be the render position, or should the identity map stay?**

*Axes.* A positional key is what the TSDoc promises and fixes duplicate primitives, but it remounts rows when a read-only array reorders; the map is stable across reorders and fails only on duplicates.

*Changes.* Positional makes S43 a two-line change. Keeping the map and deduplicating collided values keeps reorder stability but adds a counter and changes the emitted `data-item-id` shape.

**Q25 (B06-Q6) — Should the virtualized tree re-derive `defaultExpandDepth` when `data` identity changes, or converge on the recursive variant's per-render resolution?**

*Axes.* A re-seed is smaller but can clobber a user's collapses when the payload updates for another reason; per-render resolution removes the divergence the file's own comment records, at the cost of a walk every render and a rewrite of how `expanded` is stored.

*Changes.* Re-seed keeps S45 inside the virtualized file plus the expansion hook. Per-render resolution moves the open-state decision into the flatten pass, adding json-tree-utilities.tsx and changing what a controlled `expanded` set means.

**Q26 (B06-Q7) — Does the no-op-change bail live in `expand`, or in `useControllable`?**

*Axes.* `expand` is local and closes only this row; the hook is package-wide and would stop every controlled component firing a callback with an identical value — a semantics change well outside this scope, and adjacent to Q5.

*Changes.* Bailing in `expand` keeps S45's file set as listed. Bailing in the hook adds use-controllable.ts and turns S45 into a cross-cutting change needing a sweep of the hook's other callers.

**Q27 (B06-Q8) — Does JsonTree adopt the hook's index-based `itemSource`, or keep real roving focus and bound Home/End and the wrap?**

*Axes.* `itemSource` requires virtual mode with `data-active`/`aria-activedescendant` while focus stays elsewhere — a different a11y model from today's real-focus rows — and needs a `scrollToIndex` the current window call does not expose. Keeping real focus means the handler must reach unmounted rows.

*Changes.* Adopting it changes the focus model and touches all three files in S46. Bounding the keys keeps the model but makes Home/End and the wrap no-ops at the window edge, a weaker promise than the TSDoc makes.

**Q28 (B06-Q9) — Does the grand total fold incrementally, or is it computed only when `totals` asks for it?**

*Axes.* An incremental fold fixes the crash for every caller but `avg` needs a running count and `min`/`max` must keep the first-of-equal-pair reading the current comment defends. Gating on `totals` removes the cost for callers that never render one but changes the published result shape.

*Changes.* Fold keeps S48 in pivot-table-pivot.ts alone. Gating adds use-pivot-table.ts and pivot-table.tsx and makes `grandTotal` conditionally `undefined`, contradicting its TSDoc.

**Q29 (B06-Q10) — For ListboxContext, keep the generated hook and call it, or give the context an explicit default plus a guard at the option?**

*Axes.* The factory's documented thin case pairs a discarded hook with an explicit default; the option factory reads any host context directly, so a hook cannot be forced there without changing the factory's contract for Combobox and Select too.

*Changes.* An explicit default keeps S49 in listbox/context.ts but shifts the guard into the option factory, reaching every host. The named hook keeps the change local but requires a signature change across the select family.

**Q30 (B07-Q1) — Should CollapseTrigger point `aria-controls` under `mount="lazy"` once the panel has mounted, or restrict the reference to `mount="always"` as `mountsEveryPanel` prescribes?**

*Axes.* A Collapse has one trigger and one panel behind one context, so its trigger can observe its own panel — the case the mount primitive excludes because no sibling trigger can. Weigh that against uniformity with stepper-step.tsx and the risk of a dangling id. The one shipped non-default consumer is a `lazy` Collapse.

*Changes.* Restricting keeps S34 at two trigger files and a shared helper call. Honouring `lazy` adds a presence signal to collapse/context.ts and a read in collapse-panel.tsx, and makes the chat-tool call site a live fix.

**Q31 (B07-Q2) — For a Card, is a flush header-to-body edge the intended visual, or is the projected gap correct and the TSDoc wrong?**

*Axes.* The recipe calls the header's `pb` the gap to the body; the Card and CardHeader TSDoc say the same adjacency collapses it to zero. Both cannot be right. Weigh the shipped appearance at five call sites, unchanged since the doc was written, against the doc's explicit promise.

*Changes.* Flush: S12 adds a sibling selector plus a positive test and a browser-suite geometry check, and changes the shipped look at five call sites. Gap: S12 becomes a TSDoc and test-comment edit with no visual change and no recipe touch.

**Q32 (B07-Q3) — Should AvatarGroup reach its children through React rather than through DOM projection alone?**

*Axes.* REFERENCE.md §2 makes the static tier project through the DOM and bars it from reading context, and avatar-group.tsx carries no `'use client'`. Against that, the StatusDot's size is a React prop no selector can set, so a CSS-only fix needs a second projection keyed on the dot's slot.

*Changes.* CSS-only keeps S50 inside the kata plus a test and preserves the static tier. A React channel makes AvatarGroup a client component, changing its tier and widening S50 to the barrel and the boundary tests.

**Q33 (split from B07-Q5) — What should an Avatar with `alt` but no `src` and no `initials` render?**

*Axes.* An sr-only span keeps the root a plain styled span; `role="img"` plus `aria-label` names the box itself and changes what `getByRole` finds at every existing Avatar site; a third arm makes the empty case a type error.

*Changes.* The sr-only arm touches avatar.tsx alone. The `role="img"` arm also affects the existing role queries in the avatar test and any consumer selector.

**Q34 (split from B07-Q5) — Should the Accordion be a single Tab stop?**

*Axes.* The WAI-ARIA accordion pattern the component's own TSDoc names puts every header button in the Tab sequence, while the words "roving tabindex" in accordion.tsx mean one stop. One of the two must give.

*Changes.* Passing `manageTabIndex` is a behaviour change in accordion.tsx plus a new tabIndex test. Rewording is a TSDoc edit across the three accordion files with no behaviour change.

**Q35 (B07-Q6) — Does StatDelta's third trend state rename to `flat`, or does its TSDoc adopt `neutral`?**

*Axes.* `neutral` is the recipe arm, the shipped default and what the demos pass, so renaming breaks a public prop value; `flat` is what the TSDoc has told readers to write. Weigh the public surface against how long the wrong word has been published.

*Changes.* The rename widens S53 to the recipe and the demo call sites and needs a changeset. The prose arm collapses S53 to one TSDoc line.

**Q36 (B07-Q7) — Does a hand-written multi-child skeleton carry its own `data-slot` root anchor, or is `placeholder` the family anchor?**

*Axes.* §10.2 asks every new component for a stable `data-slot`; §3.7 puts pagination in a hand-written family whose factory emits no per-skeleton anchor, so an anchor on pagination alone would be the outlier.

*Changes.* Anchoring widens S52 to every hand-written count-keyed skeleton §3.7 names, most of which sit outside this file list and need their own scope. Declaring `placeholder` the family anchor closes the row with a TSDoc note and no code change.

**Q37 (B08-Q1) — What is the viewer's document identity when the consumer supplies `pages`?**

*Axes.* Referential stability required and documented on the prop; derived from the page data; or an explicit `documentKey` prop the consumer owns.

*Changes.* Stability-required turns S58 into a TSDoc change plus an audit of every `pages` call site and sits in tension with the sibling `highlights` waiver. Derived changes both hook signatures and adds an O(pages) pass per render. An explicit prop adds public surface and pulls a docs index touch in with it.

**Q38 (B08-Q2) — Where does "loading" begin for a cold `src`?**

*Axes.* At subscription, so a registered-but-unfilled entry reports loading from the first render; or in the viewport's branch order, so `src` with no pages and no error paints the placeholder while `loading` keeps meaning "a run is in flight".

*Changes.* The first edits the cache and the frozen `EMPTY` singleton whose identity the server snapshot depends on, so it must answer for the server render. The second confines that half of S59 to the viewport and leaves the cache's vocabulary alone.

**Q39 (B08-Q3) — Does a retry on an errored entry defer to the viewers holding it?**

*Axes.* Skip the revoke and reset while listeners remain and append to the partial pages; reset without revoking and leave the URLs to the evictor; or refuse the retry while a holder renders the partial pages and retry only on a cold subscribe.

*Changes.* The first two decide whether a partially-rasterized document can briefly show its pages twice, the case the revoke exists to prevent. The third narrows the recovery path the TSDoc promises and must say what a held error recovers by instead.

**Q40 (B08-Q4) — Which node keeps the `pdf-viewer-page-status` name?**

*Axes.* The toolbar's total count keeps it, so the existing assertion stands and the live region takes a new name; or the live region keeps it as the node the name describes, and the toolbar count takes a new name.

*Changes.* It decides which file S60's rename lands in, whether the existing test changes, and which anchor a future consumer's muscle memory finds.

**Q41 (merges B08-Q5 and B09-Q1) — Does the re-announce fix land in `useA11yAnnouncements` or at the call site, and must a status that clears and returns to the same string be spoken again?**

*Axes.* Whether an absent message is a state change worth resetting the baseline or merely a gap in a continuing status: weigh a reader who re-queries and lands on the same count, who hears nothing today, against a reader watching a value flicker to null and back on every refetch. Second axis: the hook reaches every caller in the package; the call site is contained but re-states the dedupe rule locally.

*Changes.* Reset-in-hook: S30 is one edit inside use-a11y-announcements.ts and covers every future nullable-status caller, and it changes behaviour for every other caller (see the leads). Call-site: S30 drops to pdf-viewer-highlights.tsx, which would keep the last label rather than clearing it, and the hook stays as written.

**Q42 (B08-Q6) — Is the mobile thumbnails control a disclosure toggle or an opener?**

*Axes.* A toggle matching the desktop sibling and the button's own TSDoc; or an opener that stamps no `aria-expanded`, which the same TSDoc explicitly allows.

*Changes.* The toggle adds a close path to a control the modal Sheet already renders unreachable while open, duplicating the Sheet's own close button. The opener removes an attribute and one assertion and leaves the two controls deliberately different in kind, which the file should then say.

**Q43 (B09-Q2) — May `useA11yAutoFocus` change its public signature from a `RefObject` to a node-reporting shape?**

*Axes.* It is a public barrel export, so a signature change pulls the TSDoc and the matching docs index in. Weigh that against an in-place fix that keeps the `RefObject` and re-arms when the node arrives, which repeats work two siblings already solved by holding the node in state.

*Changes.* A signature change makes S31 touch the hook, the one call site, both barrels and the surface index, and aligns the hook with its two siblings. An in-place fix confines S31 to the hook plus its test and leaves two idioms for one problem.

**Q44 (B09-Q3) — Should `useSortableSensors` absorb an unstable `keyboardCoordinateGetter`, or state a stability requirement?**

*Axes.* Every getter passed today is module-scope, so the two are equivalent now; they diverge the first time a caller builds a getter per render. The pointer half already chose the memo.

*Changes.* Absorb makes S32 a `useMemo` over the options object and needs no caller change. State-the-requirement makes S32 a TSDoc line and leaves the defect live for any inline getter.

**Q45 (B10-Q2) — Is the toast lifetime per toast, or one deadline for the stack?**

*Axes.* The queue is built for a stack-wide sweep, while `ToastInput.duration` and the `'timeout'` reason describe per-toast lifetimes. Only one can be the contract.

*Changes.* Per-toast reaches use-toast-timer.ts and use-toast-queue.ts and re-opens the WCAG 2.2.1 pause accounting, which today is one counter for one timer. Stack-wide makes the arithmetic pick a deadline deliberately (the minimum remaining rather than the newest) and the per-toast field's TSDoc becomes the wrong thing, a diff confined to toast.tsx.

**Q46 (B10-Q4) — In SidebarLayout, does only padding move to the `space` axis, or the desktop panel width too?**

*Axes.* The density token assigns padding and gap to `space` and text and icon to `size`; a rail's width is neither, and the layout reads one property for all four uses today.

*Changes.* Padding-only flips one pinned assertion and leaves the panel recipe's `size` variant untouched. Moving the width flips a second and renames the variant key, and decides whether the same question is owed to the other layouts that size furniture off the token.

**Q47 (B10-Q5) — Does `providers/density` split its cascade-reading hook into its own `'use client'` module, or take the directive on the whole file?**

*Axes.* `DensityProvider` is server-renderable today precisely because the file carries no directive, and REFERENCE.md protects the directive-free host pattern; the level-reading hook is a client-tier read and the provider doc already says so.

*Changes.* A split adds a file and a barrel line and keeps the provider server-renderable. A whole-file directive costs nothing for today's callers but forecloses an RSC `<DensityProvider>`, the entry point the provider doc names.

**Q48 (B10-Q6) — For a modal Overlay with `backdrop={false}`, does the outside press dismiss, or is the combination refused?**

*Axes.* The `backdrop` TSDoc frames the flag as paint-only and independent of modality, while the dismissal comment assumes a modal overlay always has a backdrop to click. Second axis: the dismissal ref is the full-viewport root, so "outside the panel" is inside the boundary unless the ref moves.

*Changes.* Dismissing means the outside-pointer flag keys on "no backdrop rendered" and the boundary ref moves from the root to the panel, which also changes how a portalled child press is judged. Refusing ties `backdrop: false` to `modal: false` in the type, closes the row with no behaviour change, and pushes the same question onto Sheet's re-exported `backdrop`.

**Q49 (B11-Q1) — What timing does the live region need for a repeated identical message, and does the announcer own re-attachment?**

*Axes.* Microtask (today) against a task boundary or a frame; and re-creating a detached region against leaving detachment to the test-only reset. The second axis turns on whether any shipped surface is expected to clear `document.body`, which today none is.

*Changes.* A task boundary makes S29 a timing change and forces fake timers in its test; keeping the microtask makes S29 a re-attachment change only and leaves the repeat row recorded. Choosing not to re-attach drops the detachment row from S29 and leaves it test-scoped. Either way S30 stays behind S29.

**Q50 (B11-Q2) — Does the product ship an RTL surface, now or on the roadmap?**

*Axes.* Ships or does not. No `rtl` appears in apps/admin, apps/places or the demos, yet the helper already carries an RTL branch, so the intent exists in code.

*Changes.* If RTL ships, S67 rises in the order and the row's cost is live, because the false positive blocks dismissal on every bordered scroller. If not, S67 stays a latent correction whose new test file is the only guard.

**Q51 (B11-Q3) — Does the `id` formatter's contract cover non-finite input, or does the caller guard?**

*Axes.* The formatter handles NaN and Infinity itself against the caller guarding first, which is what the remarks tell the caller to do for the numeric specs; a third axis is whether the two branches of the spec may differ here at all.

*Changes.* Handling it makes S68 a change to `formatId` plus two test cases. Leaving it to the caller makes S68 test-only and the row becomes a documented divergence rather than a defect.

**Q52 (B11-Q4) — Between an extra and a slot of the same name, which wins, and should the clash be an error?**

*Axes.* Throw (extending the guard's documented set), let the slot win (assign extras first), or keep last-writer-wins as an unstated feature. No shipped kata exercises any of the three.

*Changes.* Throwing makes S69 a guard widening plus a test case and updates the `@throws` set. Slot-wins is a two-line reorder. Keeping today's order closes the row with no code change.

**Q53 (B11-Q5) — How far does "best-effort" extend for `aria-labelledby`?**

*Axes.* Resolve the first ID in the list, join every resolved target's text as the ARIA algorithm does, or hold the single-ID read; weigh against the helper's own disclaimer that it is not the full algorithm.

*Changes.* Joining makes S70 a loop plus a test; first-ID-only makes it a `split` plus a test; holding closes the row with no code change and records the list case as out of scope.

**Q54 (B11-Q6) — For the three unreached helper guarantees, harden the runtime or hold the rows as recorded?**

*Axes.* Cost of the change (one or two lines each) against the value of a guarantee no caller exercises; and whether the package wants runtime freezing at all, given `as const` is the house idiom and referential stability is the tested property.

*Changes.* Hardening runs S71 as one commit with two new test files. Holding drops S71 and leaves the three rows recorded and unreached; a middle outcome (harden `once` and `memoWeak`, hold the freeze) narrows S71 to the two utilities and their tests.

## Ruled out

54 entries the blind pass killed, each with the guard that killed it. A later sweep that raises one of these has produced a known false claim.

- `B01-C09 — the dropped part: "when the stepped year falls outside the visible decade no cell is marked at all and focus drops to the grid's first cell"` — calendar-picker-reducer.ts:50 `case 'showYears': return { ...state, view: 'years', decadeYear: state.pickerYear }` — entering the years view anchors the decade on the stepped year itself, and use-calendar-picker.tsx:90 `const decadeStart = Math.floor(decadeYear / 10) * 10` with :164 `const y = decadeStart - 1 + i` then always renders it, so the stepped year cannot fall outside the visible decade on that transition. The unmarked-grid outcome is real but with the roles reversed: it occurs when the CALENDAR's `year` falls outside the decade anchored on `pickerYear` (step twelve or more years, or page the decade with :205-206 `onPrev`/`onNext`), because :169 `selected: y === year` marks the calendar's year. Note also that the decade-paging buttons do not call `focusPickerGrid`, so the focus drop belongs to the `showYears` transition only.
- `B02-C14 (REFUTED — whole claim)` — packages/ui/src/hooks/use-controllable.ts:66-67 ` // §7.3: the public callback reports a cleared value as `null` — echoing` / ` // `undefined` back into `value` would read as uncontrolled.` above ` onValueChangeRef.current?.(normalized ?? null)`. Reinforced by the prop's own signature one line below the claimed contract — packages/ui/src/components/number-input/number-input.tsx:29 ` onValueChange?: (value: number | null) => void` — which admits `null` and not `undefined`, by packages/ui/src/components/form/use-form-value.ts:12 ` /** Fires with the committed value, or `null` once it is cleared (CONVENTIONS §7.3). */`, and by CONVENTIONS.md:77 §7.3. The runtime behaviour is deliberate; the one stale line is number-input.tsx:28, surfaced as lead L4 rather than raised as a finding (§5.3).
- `B02-C09 (NARROWED — dropped part: "the field is left in the mixed controlled/uncontrolled state that warning describes")` — packages/ui/src/components/input/use-input-value.ts:52 ` value: bound ? binding.value : own,` with :47 ` const bound = value === undefined && binding !== undefined`, and packages/ui/src/components/form/use-form-text.ts:46 ` value: typeof field.value === 'string' ? field.value : '',` — a bound Input always supplies a defined string `value`, so React takes the controlled path and the stray `defaultValue` never drives the rendered value. The confirmed part is the logged error and the attribute on the element; the rendered state stays consistent with the store.
- `B02-C10 (NARROWED — dropped part: "an inconsistent initial render")` — Same guard as B02-C09, applied through the shared hook: packages/ui/src/components/input/use-input-value.ts:52 ` value: bound ? binding.value : own,`, reached from packages/ui/src/components/textarea/textarea.tsx:59-64 ` const valueState = useInputValue<HTMLTextAreaElement>({` / ` name,` / ` value,` / ` onChange,` / ` onBlur,` / ` })` and written at :94 ` value: valueState.value,`. The initial render shows the bound value; only the error log and the stray attribute are reached.
- `B02-C15 (NARROWED — dropped part: "the mismatch is not reliably announced" as an absolute)` — A second, working announcement channel is wired and test-pinned. packages/ui/src/components/password-confirm/password-confirm.tsx:62 ` const warningId = warning ? generatedWarningId : undefined` feeds the context at :65, which the confirm field consumes at packages/ui/src/components/password-confirm/password-confirm-input.tsx:48 ` const describedBy = useAriaIds(ariaDescribedBy, showWarning ? warningId : undefined)` and :57 ` aria-describedby={describedBy}`. The test states the intent: packages/ui/src/__tests__/components/password-confirm.test.tsx:65-66 ` // The warning id joins the input's aria-describedby, so focusing the` / ` // invalid field announces the reason, not a bare "invalid".` What is confirmed is narrower — the polite live-region channel never fires, because the region mounts with its content.
- `B02-C03 (NARROWED trigger, recorded for completeness — the claim's "17-or-more-digit" bound)` — No guard exists; the bound is wider than claimed, not narrower. packages/ui/src/components/currency-input/currency-input-utilities.ts:50 ` const n = Number(trimmed)` is lossy from 2^53 (9007199254740992) upward, so 16-digit runs above that value are already rewritten. Recorded here so the verified trigger is not read as the claim's.
- `B03-C12` — REFUTED. The `!multiple` carve-out is pinned as deliberate by a test: packages/ui/src/__tests__/components/use-combobox-input.test.ts:113 ` it('does not clear in multi-select mode', () => {`, set up at :114-118 with `clearOnEmpty: true`, `multiple: true`, `value: ['x']`, and asserted at :124 ` expect(setValue).not.toHaveBeenCalled()`. The paired single-select test names the scope in its own title at :93 ` it('clears the value when clearOnEmpty is true and the input goes empty in single-select', () => {`. The claim's contract (the TSDoc at combobox.tsx:118, which states no carve-out) describes a documentation gap; §5.5 bars me from proposing a document change, and §3.7 requires REFUTED when a test shows deliberate behaviour.
- `B03-C01 (dropped part)` — NARROWED. The part dropped is the claim's persistence — "The visual state and the accessibility state disagree from that click onward, for as long as the `indeterminate` prop stays `true`." The one site in the consumer roots never holds the prop true across an activation. Guard: packages/ui/src/modules/grid/use-grid-selection.ts:90 ` const every = keys.length > 0 && keys.every((k) => current.has(k))` and :92 ` return every ? new Set() : new Set(keys)` — every activation moves the selection to all-or-none, so `indeterminate={someSelected && !allSelected}` (packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-head.tsx:189) evaluates false in the same commit and the effect at checkbox.tsx:75-77 re-runs. The confirmed part is the mechanism: with the dependency array `[indeterminate]`, nothing restores a still-true prop's DOM property after activation clears it.
- `B03-C13 / B03-C14 (reach, not the mechanism)` — Not a refutation — recorded so the reader does not mistake the CONFIRMED verdicts for reachable defects. No consumer in the roots passes `role` to either wrapper: packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/radio.tsx:12, :23, :34 pass only `aria-label`; packages/ui/src/docs/demos/components/checkbox.tsx:19, :35, :46 the same; packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-column-manager.tsx:221, :282 and packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-group-manager.tsx:517, :585 pass nothing. The trigger is constructible (the type at checkbox-group.tsx:5 and radio-group.tsx:11-12 admits `role`), so §3.6's refutation arm does not apply; the rows stand with reach NONE.
- `B04-C12` — REFUTED — two independent guards. No in-repo\n\t// consumer composes them that way.` And the public signatures refuse the composition: context-menu-merge.ts:12 `export function mergeContextMenuItems(groups: ContextMenuEntry[][]): ContextMenuEntry[] {` against :31-34 `export function resolveContextMenuEntries(\n\tconfig: ContextMenuConfig | undefined,\n\tdefaults: ContextMenuItem[],\n): ContextMenuEntry[] {` — a `ContextMenuEntry[]` is not assignable to `ContextMenuItem[]`, because packages/ui/src/components/context-menu/types.ts:32-36 `export type ContextMenuSeparator = {` … `separator: true\n}` supplies no `label` and types.ts:18 `label: ReactNode` is required. The same guard sits on the component prop: packages/ui/src/components/context-menu/context-menu.tsx:15 `defaults?: ContextMenuItem[]`.
- `B04-C13 (dropped part)` — NARROWED — the flip is confirmed, but "jumps to the shadow internal value (step 0)" is guarded whenever the owner drove the value through the widget. packages/ui/src/hooks/use-controllable.ts:59-63 `// Written even while controlled: \`value !== undefined\` decides\n\t\t// controlled-ness per render, so a controlled consumer that clears to\n\t\t// \`undefined\` flips the hook to uncontrolled; the shadow keeps that\n\t\t// flip resolving to the last committed value instead of a stale one.\n\t\tsetInternalValue(normalized)`. Every step click routes through that setter (`stepper.tsx:137 onValueChange: interactive ? setCurrent : undefined`), so the shadow already holds the last committed index and the stepper stays where it was. Step 0 appears only where the shadow was never written — an owner that moved `value` programmatically and never let the widget commit — so the "jumps to step 0" half is the narrower case, not the general one. The confirmed part is the silent controlled-to-uncontrolled flip and the absence of any `null` arm (`stepper.tsx:17 value?: number`).

Every consumer-root site takes this branch (see the C01 reach entry), which is why the claim's reach is NONE.
- `B04-C11 (arrangement-dependent part)` — The worked example — "the second press lands on the trailing anchor instead of the adjacent button" — is not traced as stated, because under the same grouping the resting stop is itself mis-seated: packages/ui/src/hooks/a11y/use-a11y-roving.ts:350 `return bySelector ?? items[0]` puts the initial `tabIndex=0` on the first grouped match (the anchor), not on the DOM-first button, so "from the first control" does not start where the claim assumes. What traces is the general form: the item order is grouped by selector rather than by document position, and the resting stop follows that wrong order. The production arm is refuted outright — a spec-conforming `querySelectorAll` returns document order, so the defect exists only under jsdom/nwsapi, as the repository's own caveat at use-a11y-roving.ts:54-55 records.

- `B07-C09` (DELIBERATE) — packages/ui/src/components/code/code-block.tsx:78-79 — "The highlighted `<pre>` is made non-focusable (`tabindex=\"-1\"`) to keep scroll containers out of the tab order." The stated goal is exactly the shipped result, and the code executes it on both branches: :116-120 installs a shiki transformer `pre(node) { node.properties.tabindex = '-1' }`, overriding shiki's own `tabindex=\"0\"` default, and :149 `<pre className={cn(k.block.fallback)} tabIndex={-1}>` does the same for the plain fallback. Keeping the code region out of the tab order is documented intent, not an oversight.
- `B10-C01` (KNOWN) — packages/ui/docs/audits/2026-07-13-PROPS-AUDIT.md:136 (REPLACE table, status "◯ OPEN"): "| DashboardLayout | `filters` | layouts/dashboard.tsx:18 | Single-region prop bag → compound child. Adjacent bug found: the \"beside the main column\" contract renders as a `<Stack>` column (dashboard.tsx:34) — aside and main stack; a `Flex` row was evidently intended | ◯ OPEN |". The same defect is restated at :170: "DashboardLayout's aside/main region renders stacked where its contract says \"beside\" (`layouts/dashboard.tsx:34-46`) — likely a `Stack`-for-`Flex` slip." The row names this file, this mechanism (Stack-for-Flex) and this wrong result (aside and main stack), and the audit is still a living record (:3 "Living record — resolve rows in place with the commit."), so the row is open, not near-miss. The layout contract it breaks is also in the surface doc: packages/ui/docs/LAYOUTS.md:34 "filters sit beside the main column on desktop".

## Surfaced, not judged

28 leads. A sweeper records a defect it sees in a file outside its own list, and it never judges one. A lead is not a finding and it closes no row.

- `B03` → `packages/ui/src/hooks/use-deferred-toggle.ts` — `toggle` resolves multi-select membership with `arr.includes(newValue)` and `arr.filter((v) => v !== newValue)` (lines 50-52), i.e. reference identity. A Combobox/Listbox over object options whose parent re-creates the option objects between renders can never deselect a picked item — the second click appends a second entry instead of removing the first. The hook's TSDoc says only "toggling adds / removes entries" and states no identity requirement.
- `B03` → `packages/ui/src/components/input/input.tsx` — `{...rest}` is spread last (line 112), after `type`, `id`, `name`, `value={valueState.value}`, `onChange`, `onBlur` and `{...validation}` — the inverse of the §3.9 order that `switch.tsx` sets. `InputProps` omits `value` but not `defaultValue`, so `<Form defaultValues={{a:'x'}}><Input name="a" defaultValue="y" /></Form>` puts `defaultValue` on the same element as the bound `value` (React controlled/uncontrolled error), and the bound field does not ignore `defaultValue` as CONVENTIONS §7.2 requires.
- `B04` → `packages/ui/src/hooks/a11y/use-a11y-roving.ts` — `queryItems` (lines 61-65) returns `Array.from(container.querySelectorAll(selector))` with no document-position sort, while `rowControls` in the same file (lines 46-58) sorts precisely because "jsdom (nwsapi) groups them by selector". Any `useA11yRoving` caller that passes a multi-selector `itemSelector` therefore roves out of DOM order under jsdom. Also: `handleActivationKey` (line 595) bails unless `ctx.isVirtual`, so `activationKey` is silently inert for every focus-mode roving surface.
- `B04` → `packages/ui/src/primitives/popover/popover.tsx` — `PopoverPanel`'s autofocus `useLayoutEffect` (lines 105-117) depends on `[autoFocus, itemSelector, scrollWithin]` but not on the rendered rows, so a panel whose options mount a commit later (a virtualized or async list) never seats focus on the selected row and drops it on the panel container instead. The panel also hardcodes `mode: 'focus'` roving, which makes the `activationKey` contract unreachable for every menu that roves by real focus.
- `B04` → `packages/ui/src/primitives/polymorphic/polymorphic-static.tsx` — The `render` arm calls `cloneElement(render, linkProps, children)` (line 70) where `linkProps` always contains `className` (line 65). cloneElement merges the config over the element's own props, so a call site writing `render={<Link className="…" />}` has its className silently replaced by the host's rather than merged, unlike every other className path in the library, which goes through `cn`.
- `B05` → `packages/ui/src/recipes/kata/sheet.ts` — `handle.area` (L94-105) carries `absolute inset-y-0`, and the `handle.side` entries for `top` (L109) and `bottom` (L110) add only the opposite edge, so the grab area resolves to top:0;bottom:0;left:0;right:0 and covers the whole panel. The side entries need `top-auto`/`bottom-auto`, or `inset-y-0` belongs in the `right`/`left` entries only.
- `B05` → `packages/ui/src/recipes/kata/sheet.ts` — The `width` axis (L57, defaults L71) applies `shaku.panel`'s `sm:max-w-*` to every `side`; only `full` and `fit` are compounded to `right`/`left` (L62-69). A `top`/`bottom` sheet therefore caps at `sm:max-w-md` instead of spanning the screen, contradicting the axis comment at L52-56 and the SheetProps.width TSDoc.
- `B05` → `packages/ui/src/recipes/kata/progress.ts` — `ProgressBarFillVariants` (L106) is exported with no TSDoc and no `@internal` tag, unlike the two neighbours on L104-105 and L107-108; CONVENTIONS.md §12.1 wants one or the other.
- `B06` → `packages/ui/src/hooks/use-deferred-toggle.ts` — `commit` calls `freeze(value)` with the PRE-toggle value before `toggle(newValue)`, so `selectionValue` paints the previously selected row throughout the panel's ~300ms exit animation. The hook TSDoc says the snapshot is "taken at selection time" and "holding the selected row steady during the ~300ms close" — the row that stays steady is the old one, so a single-select Listbox shows the check mark on the wrong option while it closes.
- `B06` → `packages/ui/src/primitives/option/option.tsx` — `createSelectOption`'s generated `Option` reads its host state with a bare `use(config.context)` and never guards the result. When the host context was built by `createContext(name)` with no `{ default }`, the value outside a provider is the MISSING sentinel Symbol, which destructures to four undefineds; `handleSelect` then calls `onSelect(value)` and throws a bare TypeError. Applies to every host built on this factory, not just Listbox.
- `B06` → `packages/ui/src/primitives/control/control.tsx` — `ControlFrame` accepts and spreads arbitrary props (including `onClick` handed down through `SelectTrigger.frameProps`) but has no disabled handling, and its recipe (recipes/kiso/control/frame.ts) sets no `pointer-events-none`. Any select-family host that routes its open toggle through `frameProps` stays clickable on the frame chrome while its inner control is disabled.
- `B06` → `packages/ui/src/hooks/use-controllable.ts` — `setValue` fires `onValueChangeRef.current?.(normalized ?? null)` unconditionally, with no comparison against the value already held. Any consumer whose updater deliberately returns the previous value to signal "no change" still emits a change notification — the mechanism behind B06-C14, but it affects every control built on the hook.
- `B07` → `packages/ui/src/recipes/kata/avatar.ts` — The AvatarGroup projection set is inconsistent in selector depth: `group.ring` (line 33) uses the direct-child variant `*:ring-2 *:ring-white`, while `group.size` (lines 45-49) uses the descendant variant `**:data-[slot=avatar]:size-*` precisely because, per its own comment, `**:` is needed to reach "an avatar inside its with-status wrapper". The ring therefore lands on the un-rounded `data-slot=avatar-with-status` span. There is also no projected row for `data-slot=status-dot`, so a non-md group leaves the corner dot at its md size.
- `B07` → `packages/ui/src/recipes/kata/card.ts` — `slots` (lines 14-30) carries only header `pb-*`, footer `pt-*`, and footer `gap-*`. It has no rule collapsing the header's bottom padding when a `data-slot=card-body` is its next sibling, although both `card.tsx` and `card-header.tsx` document that collapse as guaranteed behaviour. A `*:data-[slot=card-header]:has(+[data-slot=card-body]):pb-0` row per size step is the missing piece.
- `B07` → `packages/ui/src/primitives/polymorphic/fallback.tsx` — `renderFallback` writes `type={as === 'button' ? 'button' : undefined}` at line 57, before `{...rest}` at line 59. Every polymorphic button in the library (PaginationPage, Group's button arm, and any future `as="button"` host) can therefore be flipped to a form submit by a stray consumer `type` prop — the exact failure CONVENTIONS.md §3.9 names. The same ordering issue is reported separately for accordion-trigger.tsx and collapse-trigger.tsx, but this is not their shared root: both render a raw `<button>` and neither imports the polymorphic primitives, so the three writes are independent. This lead covers the polymorphic family only — `Badge`, `PaginationPage` and `Group`'s button arm — where one reorder does fix every `as="button"` host at once.
- `B07` → `packages/ui/src/components/pagination/pagination-utilities.tsx` — `PaginationNavButton` (line 14) writes `type="button"` and `variant="plain"` before `{...props}`, so PaginationNext/PaginationPrevious can be turned into form-submit buttons by a spread prop (CONVENTIONS.md §3.9). The file is in scope B07's list, but the symbol is `@internal` and not barrelled, so the reachable trigger runs through pagination-next.tsx / pagination-previous.tsx.
- `B08` → `packages/ui/src/hooks/a11y/use-a11y-announcements.ts` — The baseline ref is not updated on the falsy-message branch: the effect returns early when `message` is null/undefined *before* writing `previous.current`. A caller that alternates message -> null -> the same message (the pdf-viewer highlight overlay does exactly this on select/clear/re-select) gets no announcement for the repeat.
- `B08` → `packages/ui/src/components/radio/radio.tsx` — `{...props}` is spread onto the `<input>` after `type="radio"`, `data-slot`, `disabled`, `required` and the resolved `validation` attributes, so a stray prop can change the input type or clobber the widget ARIA state. CONVENTIONS.md §3.9 requires those to be written after the spread.
- `B08` → `packages/ui/src/components/checkbox/checkbox.tsx` — Same spread-order issue: `{...props}` lands after `type="checkbox"`, `data-slot`, the resolved `validation` attributes and `className`, so a caller-supplied `aria-invalid` or `className` overrides the component's own resolved wiring rather than merging (CONVENTIONS.md §3.9).
- `B09` → `packages/ui/src/core/accessible-name.ts` — `accessibleName` reads `aria-labelledby` and passes the whole attribute value to `getElementById`. ARIA allows a space-separated id list, so `aria-labelledby="a b"` resolves to null and the function silently falls back to the element's own textContent, contradicting its stated "the trimmed text of its `aria-labelledby` target". Reached from use-typeahead.ts, where it decides which item a type-ahead keypress jumps to.
- `B09` → `packages/ui/src/core/announcer.ts` — `announce` clears the live region then writes the message in a `queueMicrotask`. Two announcements in the same tick queue two microtasks against the same node, so the second overwrites the first before any observation and the first message is never spoken. Reached from use-a11y-announcements.ts.
- `B09` → `packages/ui/src/utilities/keyboard-navigation.ts` — `nextIndexForKey` handles Home/End before the grid branch, returning index 0 and `itemCount - 1` even when `cols` is set, so in a grid Home/End jump to the first/last cell of the whole grid rather than of the current row (APG grid pattern reserves Ctrl for the whole-grid move). Consumed by `useA11yRoving`'s `handleMainAxisNav`.
- `B10` → `packages/ui/src/components/stack/stack.tsx` — `Stack` spreads `{...props}` after `direction="col"`, so the column is only a default; its own TSDoc records the unresolved contract in a public doccomment ("Unresolved by design, pending a maintainer call: Flex's own doc says 'use Flex for rows, Stack for columns', while `direction` here stays overridable and eight demos pass `direction="row"`"). `DashboardLayout` consumes it as if it laid out a row.
- `B10` → `packages/ui/src/hooks/use-virtual-window.ts` — `scrollToIndex: virtualizer.scrollToIndex` is returned unwrapped (line 93) and the resync `useEffect` at lines 79-81 has no dependency array, so it runs after every commit. If the virtualizer re-binds `scrollToIndex` between renders, `VirtualOptions`' `source` memo (virtual-options.tsx:144-154) invalidates each render and its registration effect (:158-166) tears down and re-writes `registryRef.current` on every commit, briefly nulling the roving owner's item source.
- `B10` → `packages/ui/src/hooks/use-offcanvas.ts` — The `--breakpoint-lg` auto-close subscribes inside a `useEffect` with `[]` deps (lines 35-49) and reads the token exactly once via `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)`. A theme swap that changes the token leaves the listener bound to the stale media query, and the bail on an undefined token is permanent for the component's lifetime — both silently drop the auto-close that `SidebarLayout` and `DashboardLayout` document in their `onOpenChange` TSDoc.
- `B10` → `packages/ui/src/components/button/button.tsx` — `sharedProps` (`data-slot`, `data-variant`, `data-size`, `data-has-prefix`, `data-has-suffix`, `data-has-label`, lines 130-137) is written before `{...buttonProps}` / `{...props}` with the comment "consumer `props` spread later can still override". `data-has-label` and `data-size` are load-bearing wiring the button recipe keys its `data-[has-label]` selectors on, which CONVENTIONS §3.9 places after the spread.
- `S1` (caller) — Ten leaves write a `data-slot` the library itself selects on, but write it *before* `{...props}`, so a consumer rename takes the anchor away and the selector that reads it finds nothing: `badge` (badge.tsx), `control` and `field` (primitives/toggle/toggle.tsx), `control-frame` (primitives/control/control.tsx), `description`, `field`, `label` and `message` (components/fieldset/*), `field` (switch/switch-field.tsx), and `list-item-content` (list/list-item.tsx). Found by scanning every literal `data-slot` write against the 49 anchors the library selects on, while settling Q1. `nav-item-inner` and `sidebar-item-inner` share the shape and are already rows (`B04-C07`, `B04-C08`, step S5); these ten are not. Six further leaves lock an anchor no selector reads — `address-input`, `hold-button`, `menu-item`, `switch`, `toggle-icon-button` — which costs a wrapper the re-anchor but breaks nothing today. `spread-order-boundary.test.ts` now computes this set on every run and holds the twelve in an allowlist, so no new instance can land; a step that fixes one deletes its entry.
- `B11` → `packages/ui/src/__tests__/boundary/internal-barrel-boundary.test.ts` — The `barrelExports` scanner uses `/export\s*\{([^}]*)\}/g`, which does not match `export type { … } from './x'` because `type` sits between `export` and the brace. Every type-only re-export in a barrel is therefore invisible to the check, so an `@internal` *type* on a barrel passes the gate. `packages/ui/src/types/index.ts` is composed almost entirely of `export type { … }` lines and is effectively unscanned.

- `B02` (verifier) — L1 — `packages/ui/src/__tests__/boundary/internal-barrel-boundary.test.ts:20` `const LAYERS = ['core', 'hooks', 'layouts', 'primitives', 'providers', 'types', 'utilities']` omits `components`, `modules`, and `recipes`, so the CONVENTIONS.md §12.1 gate covers seven source layers and no component barrel. B02-C13 is one instance found by reading one directory; the gap is package-wide and no single file shows it. A cross-component seam for its own probe, not a row in this sheet.
- `B02` (verifier) — L2 — `packages/ui/src/components/currency-input/currency-input.tsx:142` ` {...props}` lands after `type="text"` (:99), `inputMode="decimal"` (:100), and the composed `onKeyDown` (:107), so a consumer prop can replace the Enter-to-commit wiring and the input mode as well as the class B02-C02 names. Adjacent to B02-C02 in the same file, and the same §3.9 question S2 settles; surfaced rather than judged because no root passes any of the three.
- `B02` (verifier) — L3 — Spread order is not uniform across the input wrappers of this scope: `packages/ui/src/components/address-input/address-input.tsx:128` ` {...props}` comes FIRST, before `name`, `value`, and `aria-label`, while `packages/ui/src/components/currency-input/currency-input.tsx:142`, `packages/ui/src/components/number-input/number-input.tsx:178`, and `packages/ui/src/components/mask-input/mask-input.tsx:66` all put it LAST. The family has no single convention, and which end is right differs per attribute under §3.9. A cross-component seam.
- `B02` (verifier) — L4 — `packages/ui/src/components/number-input/number-input.tsx:28` ` /** Fires with the parsed value, or `undefined` when the field is cleared. */` contradicts the type on the next line and the §7.3 behaviour the code deliberately implements (see the B02-C14 refutation). An adjacent doc defect the caller can route back to a sweep; not judged here.
- `B02` (verifier) — L5 — `packages/ui/src/components/fieldset/message.tsx:136-138` ` {keyed.map(({ key, value }) => (` / ` <li key={key}>{value}</li>` / ` ))}` gives the multi-error list items no `data-slot` anchor, so CONVENTIONS.md §10.2 ("Component tests render through the library's test renderer and query by `data-slot`") has nothing to query per error. Adjacent to B02-C07 in the same branch.
- `B02` (verifier) — L6 — `packages/ui/src/components/mask-input/use-mask-input.ts:59` ` defaultValue: defaultValue !== undefined ? format(defaultValue) : '',` passes `''` rather than `undefined` when no seed is given, so an unbound, uncontrolled MaskInput starts with a defined internal value from mount. Whether that is the intended §7.3 posture is a separate question from B02-C04's controlled arm; surfaced, not judged.
- `B01` (verifier) — date-input.tsx computes `resolvedDisabled`/`resolvedReadOnly` at :177-179 by folding in Control context, then passes the raw props to the Input at :208-209 `disabled={disabled}` / `readOnly={readOnly}` while spending the resolved pair only on the suffix gate at :215-216. Whether Input re-resolves the cascade itself was not traced. Adjacent to B01-C02 but in a dependency file this scope only reads.
- `B01` (verifier) — packages/ui/src/__tests__/components/calendar.test.tsx drives the CalendarPicker popover in the jsdom suite (:219, :233, :277 `await user.click(openPicker(/June 2025/))`, then clicks inside the portalled surface), which is the floating-ui lifecycle CONVENTIONS.md:104 (§10.3) names. Any gate written for B01-C09 must choose between following that precedent and taking the hook seam S1 names. A test-architecture seam no single file shows.
- `B01` (verifier) — Under `chips: false` the same collision B01-C07 and B01-C08 describe surfaces as a wrong count rather than a wrong chip: relativeSummary (date-picker-relative-utilities.ts:310-316) reads `${chips.length} selected` over the un-collapsed span list, so two spans the user sees as one selection read as "2 selected". That is the configuration apps/places actually ships (place-filters.tsx:152 `relative={{ chips: false }}`), and no claim in this batch covers it.
- `B03` (verifier) — `ColorHexInput` (packages/ui/src/components/color/color-hex-input.tsx) is rendered by ColorPanel at color-panel.tsx:99 and is the third instance of G4's seam — another `<Input>` sub-part inside a panel that would self-resolve an enclosing Field. No claim covers it; I did not open the file or judge it.
- `B03` (verifier) — `useSignaturePadState`'s `commit` wrapper also calls `setTouched()` while `disabled` or `readOnly`, because those guards live in the callee (use-signature-pad-drawing.ts:55 ` if (disabled || readOnly) return`) and not in the wrapper (use-signature-pad-state.ts:136-140). Adjacent to B03-C03 and likely closed by the same change, but it is a distinct defect and no claim names it.
- `B03` (verifier) — `useRangePointer` takes pointer capture at use-range-pointer.ts:123 and never releases it; its named model does, at use-color-drag.ts:86-88 ` if (event.currentTarget.hasPointerCapture(event.pointerId)) {` / ` event.currentTarget.releasePointerCapture(event.pointerId)`. `endDrag` (:182-185) clears only the two refs. A third divergence in the same handler family as G3.
- `B03` (verifier) — RangeSlider never calls `useControl()` at all, so an enclosing `<Field disabled>` or `<Control disabled>` does not reach it, where Slider resolves `disabled` through `useControlProps` (slider.tsx:88-93). This is a cross-component seam (the whole control cascade versus the one control that opted out of it) that no single file in this scope shows; B03-C06 and B03-C07 touch two symptoms of it, not the boundary.
- `B03` (verifier) — `Checkbox` spreads rest last (checkbox.tsx:105), so `role` and `defaultValue` reach the `<input>` on the same path `defaultChecked` does. `type` is protected by the `Omit` at :25 but `role` is not. Same §3.9 family as G1 and probably closed by S4, though no claim covers a `role` override on `<Checkbox>` itself.
- `B03` (verifier) — React context crosses a portal, so the `<Field>` in B03-C11's trigger reaches ColorPicker's panel too — ColorPickerContent portals the ColorPanel (color-picker.tsx:134-143) while the context provider chain stays intact. G4's blast radius is wider than the inline panel, and the popover path would need the same fix.
- `B04` (verifier) — `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-manager-color-menu.tsx:53` passes `aria-label={`Color menu for ${label}`}` to `Menu`, but `MenuProps` (packages/ui/src/components/menu/menu.tsx:15-42) declares no `aria-label` — the prop is either a JSX excess-property error or silently dropped on a `display: contents` wrapper. This is the call site the prior-art row `[SURFACED] \`Menu\` — it silently drops \`aria-label\` (a call site passes one today)` names. Adjacent to B04-C03; not judged.
- `B04` (verifier) — `MenuTrigger`'s cloned-child branch writes only five attributes after `getReferenceProps` (packages/ui/src/components/menu/menu-trigger.tsx:126-129); a cloned child's own `type`, `role`, or `tabIndex` still reaches the DOM unchanged. Whether a cloned child's explicit prop should be honoured (the consumer chose the element) or overridden (the trigger owns the wiring) is a cross-component question the file alone does not settle — `TooltipTrigger` and `PopoverTrigger` are named in the same comment as the shared pattern.
- `B04` (verifier) — `Button` destructures `type` (packages/ui/src/components/button/button.tsx:94) and re-emits it at :178 before `{...buttonProps}` at :180, so `type` can never be pinned by a caller that routes through `Button`: NavItem's and SidebarItem's `type="button"` is a default the consumer overrides no matter where the caller puts it in its own JSX. Any §3.9 fix for the nav/tab/sidebar family therefore depends on `Button`'s ordering, not the caller's — a cross-component seam wider than S1.
- `B04` (verifier) — `packages/ui/src/components/stepper/stepper.tsx:98` `onValueChange: (next) => onValueChange?.(next ?? 0)` coerces a cleared value to `0` before the owner sees it, so the public callback can never report "no current step" even if the hook below it does. Adjacent to B04-C13; not judged.
- `B04` (verifier) — `Stepper`'s interactivity gate (`stepper.tsx:104` `const interactive = onValueChange !== undefined || defaultValue !== undefined`) and `StepperStep`'s button/div branch (`stepper-step.tsx:123` `if (onValueChange !== undefined) {`) read two different things — the root's own prop versus the context value the root computes at `:137`. - `B04` (verifier) — `useA11yRoving`'s main item query is unsorted for every consumer (`use-a11y-roving.ts:61-65`, called at `:403` and `:891`) while `rowControls` sorts (`:46-58`).
